, we have
a_{0},a_{1},\cdots,a_{S}
are orthonormal, and hence

\displaystyle\Delta_{x}^{(k)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{S}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbbm{1}\{n_{-1}=j\}\Bigg\{\left(1-\alpha^{(k)}_{(p(j),j,0),(j,1)}-\alpha^{(k)}_{(\widetilde{c}_{1}(j),j,0),(j,1)}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 34.14322pt+\sum_{q\in[S]\setminus\{j\}}\bigg(\alpha^{(k)}_{(p(q),q,0),(j,1)}+\alpha^{(k)}_{(\widetilde{c}_{1}(q),q,1),(j,1)}\bigg)^{2}\Bigg\}\Bigg].

This gives (
140a
). The relations (

) and (

) can be verified in a similar way.

For

, when
k\in[m]
,
x_{-1}^{(k-1)}=a_{n_{2^{m-k}}}=a_{p(n_{-1})}
, and hence by (

), we have

\displaystyle\Delta_{y}^{(k)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\Bigg\|a_{p(n_{-1})}-\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m}-1}\left(\alpha^{(k)}_{(i,2i,0),(n_{-1},1)}+\alpha^{(k)}_{(i,2i+1,0),(n_{-1},1)}\right)a_{i}

\displaystyle\hskip 34.14322pt-\left(\alpha^{(k)}_{(0,n_{1},0),(n_{-1},1)}+\alpha^{(k)}_{(0,n_{2^{m}},1),(n_{-1},1)}\right)a_{0}-\sum_{l=m-k+2}^{m}\alpha^{(k)}_{(n_{2^{l}},n_{2^{l-1}},1),(n_{-1},1)}a_{n_{2^{l}}}\Bigg\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{S}\mathbb{E}\Biggl\|\mathbbm{1}\{n_{-1}=j\}\Bigg\{\left(1-\alpha^{(k)}_{(p(j),j,0),(j,1)}-\alpha^{(k)}_{(p(j),s(j),0),(j,1)}\right)a_{j}-\left(\alpha^{(k)}_{(0,n_{1},0),(j,1)}+\alpha^{(k)}_{(0,n_{2^{m}},1),(j,1)}\right)a_{0}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-\sum_{q\in[S]\setminus\{j\}}\left(\alpha^{(k)}_{(q,c_{1}(q),0),(j,1)}+\alpha^{(k)}_{(q,c_{2}(q),0),(j,1)}+\alpha^{(k)}_{(q,p(q),1),(j,1)}\right)a_{q}\Bigg\}\Biggr\|^{2}_{2}.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\beta^{(k)}_{(0,n_{1},0),(n_{-1},1)}s_{b}-\beta^{(k)}_{(0,n_{2^{m}},1),(n_{-1},1)}s_{f}-\sum_{l=m-k+2}^{m}\beta^{(k)}_{(n_{2^{l}},n_{2^{l-1}},1),(n_{-1},1)}s_{f}\Bigg\|^{2}_{2}

B.2.5
Proof of Lemma

It’s easy to verify by straightforward computation that for any

,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\alpha^{(k)}_{(p,q,u),(j,v)}}{\partial a_{b}^{\top}B_{1}^{(t)}a_{c}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\alpha^{(k)}_{(p,q,u),(j,v)}\left(1-\sum_{s\in[S]\atop z\in\{0,1\}}\alpha^{(k)}_{(p,s,z),(j,v)}\right),&\text{if }b=p,c=j,\\

(215)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\alpha^{(k)}_{(p,q,u),(j,v)}}{\partial a_{b}^{\top}B_{2}^{(t)}a_{c}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\alpha^{(k)}_{(p,q,u),(j,v)}\left(1-\sum_{r\in[S]\atop z\in\{0,1\}}\alpha^{(k)}_{(r,q,z),(j,v)}\right),&\text{if }b=q,c=j,\\

(216)

and for any

, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\alpha^{(k)}_{(p,q,u),(j,v)}}{\partial s_{b}^{\top}B_{3}^{(t)}s_{c}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\alpha^{(k)}_{(p,q,u),(j,v)}\left(1-\sum_{r\in\{0,\ldots,S\}\atop s\in[S]}\alpha^{(k)}_{(r,s,z),(j,v)}\right),&\text{if }b=u,c=v,\\
-\alpha^{(k)}_{(p,q,u),(j,v)}\sum_{r\in\{0,\ldots,S\}\atop s\in[S]}\alpha^{(k)}_{(r,s,b),(j,v)},&\text{if }b\neq u,c=v,\\

), (

) and (

).

B.2.6
Proof of Lemma

Using (

) and (

), we can compute that for any

\displaystyle\delta z_{1,0,j}^{(k)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{S}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[-\left(1-w_{1}^{(k)}\right)q_{0}^{(k)}\left(1-q_{0}^{(k)}-p_{0}^{(k)}\right)+w_{0}^{(k)}q_{0}^{(k)}\left(1-q_{0}^{(k)}-p_{0}^{(k)}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(1-w_{1}^{(k)}\right)\left(w_{1}^{(k)}-q_{0}^{(k)}\right)\left(q_{0}^{(k)}+p_{0}^{(k)}\right)-w_{0}^{(k)}\left(w_{0}^{(k)}-p_{0}^{(k)}\right)\left(q_{0}^{(k)}+p_{0}^{(k)}\right)\Big|n_{-1}=j\Bigr].

\displaystyle\delta z_{1,0,j}^{(m+1)}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{S}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(w_{1}^{(m+1)}\right)^{2}w_{0}^{(m+1)}\left(-\frac{p_{0}^{(m+1)}}{w_{0}^{(m+1)}}+\frac{q_{0}^{(m+1)}}{w_{1}^{(m+1)}}\right)\Big|n_{-1}=j\right],

(220)

and for any
k\in\{m+2,\ldots,2m\}
,

\displaystyle\delta z_{1,0,j}^{(k)}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{S}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(w_{1}^{(k)}\right)^{2}w_{0}^{(k)}\left(-\frac{p_{0}^{(k)}}{w_{0}^{(k)}}+\frac{q_{0}^{(k)}}{w_{1}^{(k)}}\right)\Big|n_{-1}=j\right].

(221)

Combining (

), (
220
) and (
221
), we have (
151
). The other expressions in Lemma
13
can be computed analogously.

B.2.7
Proof of Lemma
15

We prove the lemma by induction.
First note that (
189
) is true when
t=0
by our initialization. For induction, we assume (
189
) holds for all time steps
s\leq t
(
0\leq t\leq T_{1}^{C}-1
).
Below we prove they hold at time
t+1
.

Step 1: showing (
189a
) holds at step
t+1
.

By (
184b
) and (
186
), we know that

\displaystyle\mu_{2}^{(t+1)}=\mu_{2}^{(t)}-\eta\sum_{k=1}^{2m}\left(-\delta z_{1,0,j}^{(t,k)}\right).

(222)

Thus to track the growth of
\mu_{2}
and show (
189a
) holds at step
t+1
, we need to estimate its the terms in the gradient expression (
151
).

We start from bounding the iteration-varying versions of
-\frac{q_{0}^{(k)}}{w_{1}^{(k)}}+\frac{p_{0}^{(k)}}{w_{0}^{(k)}}
for
k\in[m]
, and
-\frac{p_{0}^{(k)}}{w_{0}^{(k)}}+\frac{q_{0}^{(k)}}{w_{1}^{(k)}}
for
k\in\{1,\ldots,2m\}
. Note that for
\frac{q_{0}^{(t,k)}}{w_{1}^{(t,k)}}
,

•

when
k=1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{q_{0}^{(t,k)}}{w_{1}^{(t,k)}}=1,

(223)

where
q_{0}^{(t,k)}
and
w_{1}^{(t,k)}
are the iteration-varying versions of (
148
) and (
146
), respectively;

•

when
k\in\{2,\ldots,m+1\}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{q_{0}^{(t,k)}}{w_{1}^{(t,k)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\exp\left((1+c_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}+\nu_{2,2}^{(t)}\right)}{\exp\left((1+c_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}+\nu_{2,2}^{(t)}\right)+(k-2)\exp\left(\nu_{2,1}^{(t)}+\nu_{2,2}^{(t)}+c_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)+\exp\left(\nu_{2,1}^{(t)}+(b^{(t)}+c_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)}

\displaystyle=\left(1\pm{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\log^{2}N}{N}\right)\right)\frac{\exp\left((1+c_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)}{\exp\left((1+c_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)+(k-2)\exp\left(c_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)+\exp\left((1+c_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)}

\displaystyle=\left(1\pm{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\log^{2}N}{N}\right)\right)\frac{\exp\left(\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)}{2\exp\left(\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)+k-2}\geq\left(1\pm{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\log^{2}N}{N}\right)\right)\frac{1}{k},

(224)

where in the second line we use the fact that when
t\leq T_{1}^{C}
, we have

\exp\left(x\right)=1+{\mathcal{O}}(x)=1+{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\log^{2}N}{N}\right),

where
x=\nu_{2,1}^{(t)},\nu_{2,2}^{(t)},(1-b^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}
(note that
\mu^{(t)}_{2}\lesssim\log N
by our choice of
T_{1}^{C}
);

•

when
k\in\{m+2,\ldots,2m\}
, similarly, we have

\displaystyle\frac{q_{0}^{(t,k)}}{w_{1}^{(t,k)}}

\displaystyle=\left(1\pm{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\log^{2}N}{N}\right)\right)\frac{\exp\left((1-c_{1}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)}{\exp\left((1-c_{1}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)+(m-1)\exp\left(-c_{1}^{(t)}\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)+\exp\left((1-c_{1}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)}

\displaystyle=\left(1\pm{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\log^{2}N}{N}\right)\right)\frac{\exp\left(\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)}{2\exp\left(\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)+m-1},

(225)

where the last step follows from our induction hypothesis.

Besides, turning to
\frac{p_{0}^{(t,k)}}{w_{0}^{(t,k)}}
,

•

when
k\in[m]
:

\displaystyle\frac{p_{0}^{(t,k)}}{w_{0}^{(t,k)}}

\displaystyle=\left(1\pm{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\log^{2}N}{N}\right)\right)\frac{\exp\left((1-c_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)}{\exp\left((1-c_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)+(N-2)\exp\left(-c_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)+\exp\left((b^{(t)}-c_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)}

\displaystyle=\left(1\pm{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\log^{2}N}{N}\right)\right)\frac{\exp\left(\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)}{2\exp\left(\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)+N-2}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}},

(226)

where the last two steps follow from induction hypothesis and the choice of
T_{1}^{C}
;

•

when
k=m+1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{p_{0}^{(t,m+1)}}{w_{0}^{(t,m+1)}}

\displaystyle=\left(1\pm{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\log^{2}N}{N}\right)\right)\frac{\exp\left((2-c_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)}{(N-1)\exp\left(-c_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)+\exp\left((2-c_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)}

\displaystyle=\left(1\pm{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\log^{2}N}{N}\right)\right)\frac{\exp\left(2\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)}{N-1+\exp\left(2\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)}.

(227)

•

when
k\in\{m+2,\ldots,2m\}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{p_{0}^{(t,k)}}{w_{0}^{(t,k)}}

\displaystyle=\left(1\pm{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\log^{2}N}{N}\right)\right)\frac{\exp\left((1+c_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)}{(N-4+k-m)\exp\left(c_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)+3\exp\left((1+c_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)}

\displaystyle=\left(1\pm{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\log^{2}N}{N}\right)\right)\frac{\exp\left(\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)}{3\exp\left(\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)+N-4+k-m}.

(228)

Together, (

‣

We next bound the iteration-varying versions of the terms
\left(w_{0}^{(k)}\right)^{2}w_{1}^{(k)}
and
\left(w_{1}^{(k)}\right)^{2}w_{0}^{(k)}
in the expression of
\delta z_{1,0,j}^{(k)}
(c.f. (

)) for
k\in[m]
and
k\in\{m+1,\ldots,2m\}
, respectively. By the induction hypothesis, we have

•

When
k=1
,

\displaystyle w_{0}^{(t,1)}

\displaystyle=\left(1\pm{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\log^{2}N}{N}\right)\right)\frac{(N-2)\exp\left(-c_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)+2\exp\left((1-c_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)}{\exp\left((2+c_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)+(N-2)\exp\left(-c_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)+2\exp\left((1-c_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)},

(235)

\displaystyle w_{1}^{(t,1)}

\displaystyle=\left(1\pm{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\log^{2}N}{N}\right)\right)\frac{\exp\left((2+c_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)}{\exp\left((2+c_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)+(N-2)\exp\left(-c_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)+2\exp\left((1-c_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)}.

(236)

•

When
k\in\{2,\ldots,m\}
:

\displaystyle w_{0}^{(t,k)}

\displaystyle=\left(1\pm{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\log^{2}N}{N}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\cdot\frac{(N-2)\exp\left(-c_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)+2\exp\left((1-c_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)}{2\exp\left((1+c_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)+(k-2)\exp(c_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{2}^{(t)})+(N-2)\exp\left(-c_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)+2\exp\left((1-c_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)},

(237)

\displaystyle w_{1}^{(t,k)}

\displaystyle=\left(1\pm{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\log^{2}N}{N}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\cdot\frac{2\exp\left((1+c_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)+(k-2)\exp(c_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{2}^{(t)})}{2\exp\left((1+c_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)+(k-2)\exp(c_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{2}^{(t)})+(N-2)\exp\left(-c_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)+2\exp\left((1-c_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)}.

(238)

When
k=m+1

\displaystyle w_{0}^{(t,m+1)}

\displaystyle=\left(1\pm{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\log^{2}N}{N}\right)\right)

(239)

\displaystyle w_{1}^{(t,m+1)}

\displaystyle=\left(1\pm{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\log^{2}N}{N}\right)\right)

When

\displaystyle w_{1}^{(t,k)}

(241)

\displaystyle w_{0}^{(t,k)}

\displaystyle=\left(1\pm{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\log^{2}N}{N}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\cdot\frac{(N-4+k-m)\exp\left(c_{1}^{(t)}\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)+3\exp\left((1+c_{1}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)}{(N-4+k-m)\exp\left(c_{1}^{(t)}\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)+3\exp\left((1+c_{1}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)+2\exp\left((1-c_{1}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)+(m-1)\exp\left(-c_{1}^{(t)}\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)}.

), (

(243)

\displaystyle\left(w_{1}^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{2}\left(w_{0}^{(t,m+1)}\right)

(245)

With the above expressions, we could derive the following lemma, whose proof can be found in Appendix

.

Lemma 23
.

Under our induction hypothesis, there exist absolute constants
C,C^{\prime}>0
such that when
\eta\leq\frac{C}{Nm}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{m}\left(w_{0}^{(t,k)}\right)^{2}w_{1}^{(t,k)}\geq\left(1-{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{m}{N}\right)\right)\left(w_{1}^{(k,m+1)}\right)^{2}w_{0}^{(t,m+1)}.

(246)

By Lemma

, (

) and (

) we know that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{m}\left(-\delta z_{1,0,j}^{(k)}\right)-\delta z_{1,0,j}^{(m+1)}\gtrsim\frac{1}{S\sqrt{N}}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(w_{0}^{(k)}\right)^{2}w_{1}^{(k)}\right].

Lemma

combined with (

) and our initialization we know that for any

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(w_{0}^{(t,k)}\right)^{2}w_{1}^{(t,k)}\right]\gtrsim\frac{1}{N}.

(249)

Moreover, similar as how we prove Lemma

in Appendix

Recall (

) gives

\displaystyle\forall j\in[S]:\,\,\delta z_{1,0,j}^{(t,k)}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\frac{2}{S}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(w_{0}^{(t,k)}\right)^{2}w_{1}^{(t,k)}\left(-\frac{q_{0}^{(t,k)}}{w_{1}^{(k)}}+\frac{p_{0}^{(t,k)}}{w_{0}^{(t,k)}}\right)\Big|n_{-1}=j\right],&k\in[m]\\
\frac{2}{S}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(w_{1}^{(t,k)}\right)^{2}w_{0}^{(t,k)}\left(-\frac{p_{0}^{(t,k)}}{w_{0}^{(t,k)}}+\frac{q_{0}^{(t,k)}}{w_{1}^{(t,k)}}\right)\Big|n_{-1}=j\right],&k\in\{m+1,\ldots,2m\}.\end{cases}

Thus by (

),

B.2.8
Proof of Lemma
16

We prove by induction that (

), (

), (

), (

) and

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\log^{2}N}{N},

(269)

\displaystyle\exp\left(\left(1+2c_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)

\displaystyle\geq N

(271)

and by (

Thus we have

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{k=m+2}^{2m}\left(w_{1}^{(T_{1}^{C},k)}\right)^{2}w_{0}^{(T_{1}^{C},k)}}{\left(w_{1}^{(T_{1}^{C},m+1)}\right)^{2}w_{0}^{(T_{1}^{C},m+1)}}

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{m\exp\left(\left(2c_{2}^{(T_{1}^{C})}-1\right)\mu_{2}^{(T_{1}^{C})}\right)}{N^{2}\exp\left(2\left(2c_{1}^{(T_{1}^{C})}-1\right)\mu_{2}^{(T_{1}^{C})}\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{m\exp\left(\left(2c_{2}^{(T_{1}^{C})}+1\right)\mu_{2}^{(T_{1}^{C})}\right)}{N^{2}\exp\left(4c_{1}^{(T_{1}^{C})}\mu_{2}^{(T_{1}^{C})}\right)}\overset{\eqref{eq:1+2c_1_bound}}{\lesssim}\frac{\log^{2}N}{N},

where the last inequality also uses the fact that

Step 1: Showing (

) for

By (

) and (
•
‣
B.2.7
) and the induction hypothesis, we have

\displaystyle\frac{p_{0}^{(t,m+1)}}{w_{0}^{(t,m+1)}}

\displaystyle=\left(1\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right)\frac{\exp\left(2\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)}{N-1+\exp\left(2\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)}=\left(1\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right)\cdot\frac{2}{3},

\displaystyle\frac{q_{0}^{(t,m+1)}}{w_{1}^{(t,m+1)}}

\displaystyle=\left(1\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right)\frac{\exp\left(\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)}{2\exp\left(\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)+m-1}=\left(1\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right)\cdot\frac{1}{2},

(274)

and thus by (
151
) and the above expression, we have

\displaystyle-\delta z_{1,0,j}^{(t,m+1)}

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{2}{S}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(w_{1}^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{2}w_{0}^{(t,m+1)}\Big|n_{-1}=j\right].

(275)

By (
•
‣
B.2.7
), (
•
‣
B.2.7
) and (

), we have

-\delta z_{1,0,j}^{(t,m+1)}\geq 0
,
and by (

) we have

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{k=m+2}^{2m}\delta z_{1,0,j}^{(t,k)}\right|\lesssim\frac{\log^{2}N}{N}\left(-\delta z_{1,0,j}^{(t,m+1)}\right).

(276)

Thus we have

\displaystyle\mu_{2}^{(t+1)}\gtrsim\mu_{2}^{(t)}-\eta\delta z_{1,0,j}^{(t,m+1)}.

(277)

By (

) and (

), we have

\displaystyle\left(w_{1}^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{2}w_{0}^{(t,m+1)}

\displaystyle\asymp\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\exp\left(\left(2c_{2}^{(t)}-1\right)\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)},\,\,\text{when $c_{2}^{(t)}\geq 0.5$}\\
\frac{1}{\exp\left(2\left(1-2c_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)},\,\,\text{when $c_{2}^{(t)}\leq 0.5$}\end{cases},

(278)

and when
c_{2}^{(t)}\geq 0.5
, by (

) and (
238
), we have
\forall k\in[m]
:

\displaystyle\left(w_{0}^{(t,k)}\right)^{2}w_{1}^{(t,k)}

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{N^{2}}{\exp\left(2\left(2c_{2}^{(t)}+1\right)\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)},\;\mbox{and}\;\frac{\left(w_{0}^{(t,k)}\right)^{2}w_{1}^{(t,k)}}{\left(w_{1}^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{2}w_{0}^{(t,m+1)}}

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{N^{2}}{\exp\left(\left(2c_{2}^{(t)}+3\right)\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)}.

(279)

Following the same argument as we show (

) in Phase 1 by leveraging (

), here by leveraging (

), we can show that when

and

) and the above relation, we have

(282)

On the other hand, (

where the last step follows from our induction hypothesis.
(

C

for all

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{k=m+2}^{2m}\left(w_{1}^{(t+1,k)}\right)^{2}w_{0}^{(t+1,k)}}{\left(w_{1}^{(t+1,m+1)}\right)^{2}w_{0}^{(t+1,m+1)}}>\frac{Cm^{2}}{N},

then when

in Appendix

(300)

By (

) and (

) we have

\displaystyle-\delta z_{3,0,0}^{(t,m+1)}\gtrsim\frac{m^{2}S}{2N}\left(-\delta z_{1,0,j}^{(t,m+1)}\right)\overset{\eqref{eq:delta_z_1_m+1_dominate}}{\gtrsim}\frac{mS}{2N}\sum_{k=1}^{2m}\left(-\delta z_{1,0,j}^{(t,k)}\right),

).
The induction is complete.

B.2.9
Proof of Lemma
17

We first compute some important quantities that will be used in the proof.

Basic computations.

When
t\geq T_{2}^{C}
, by induction hypothesis and a similar computation as in Step 1 of the proof of Lemma
15
, we have when
\epsilon
is small enough:

\displaystyle w_{0}^{(t,1)}

\displaystyle\asymp\exp\left(\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\mu_{2}^{(t)}}{N}\right)\right)\frac{1}{\exp\left((1+2c_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)},\quad w_{1}^{(t,1)}\asymp 1.

(308)

and for any
k\in\{2,\ldots,m\}
:

\displaystyle w_{0}^{(t,k)}

\displaystyle\asymp\exp\left(\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\mu_{2}^{(t)}}{N}\right)\right)\frac{1}{\exp\left((2c_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)},\quad w_{1}^{(t,k)}\asymp 1.

(309)

When
k=m+1
, we have

\displaystyle w_{0}^{(t,m+1)}

\displaystyle\asymp 1,\quad w_{1}^{(t,m+1)}\asymp\exp\left(\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\mu_{2}^{(t)}}{N}\right)\right)\frac{1}{\exp\left((1-2c_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)}.

(310)

When
k\in\{m+2,\ldots,2m\}
, we have

\displaystyle w_{1}^{(t,k)}

\displaystyle\asymp\exp\left(\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\mu_{2}^{(t)}}{N}\right)\right)\frac{1}{\exp\left(2c_{1}^{(t)}\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)},\quad w_{0}^{(t,k)}\asymp 1.

(311)

The above expressions give that

\displaystyle\left(w_{0}^{(t,k)}\right)^{2}w_{1}^{(t,k)}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\exp\left(\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\mu_{2}^{(t)}}{N}\right)\right)\frac{1}{\exp\left(2(1+2c_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)},&k=1\\
\exp\left(\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\mu_{2}^{(t)}}{N}\right)\right)\frac{1}{\exp\left((4c_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)},&k\in\{2,\ldots,m\},\end{cases}

(312)

and

\displaystyle\left(w_{1}^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{2}w_{0}^{(t,m+1)}

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\mu_{2}^{(t)}}{N}\right)\right)\frac{1}{\exp\left(2(1-2c_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)}.

(313)

and

\displaystyle\forall k\in\{m+2,\ldots,2m\},\quad\left(w_{1}^{(t,k)}\right)^{2}w_{0}^{(t,k)}

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\mu_{2}^{(t)}}{N}\right)\right)\frac{1}{\exp\left(4c_{1}^{(t)}\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)}.

(314)

Below we show Lemma
17

By Lemma
16
we know that Lemma
17

holds for
t=T_{2}^{C}
.
We assume that Lemma
17
holds for all
s\leq t
(

).
Below we show that Lemma

holds for
t+1
.

Step 1: showing (

) holds for

Following the same computation as in Step 1 in the proof of Lemma

, we could compute that (
277
) holds here, i.e., we have

\displaystyle\mu_{2}^{(t+1)}\gtrsim\mu_{2}^{(t)}-\eta\delta z_{1,0,j}^{(t,m+1)},

(315)

and

\displaystyle-\delta z_{1,0,j}^{(t,m+1)}

(316)

By (
313
) and induction hypothesis we know that when
\epsilon
is small enough, we have

(317)

This together with (

) implies that

) holds for

.

Step 2: showing (
195b
), (
195c
) hold for
t+1
.

This step is almost the same as Step 2 and 3 in the proof of Lemma

and is omitted here.

Step 3: showing (
195d
) holds for
t+1
.

Similar to Step 3 in the proof of Lemma
16
, we could show by contradiction that when
\eta\lesssim\frac{1}{mN}
:

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{k=1}^{m}\left(w_{0}^{(t,k)}\right)^{2}w_{1}^{(t,k)}}{\left(w_{1}^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{2}w_{0}^{(t,m+1)}}=1\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right),

(319)

since by (
154
), if

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{m}\left(w_{0}^{(t,k)}\right)^{2}w_{1}^{(t,k)}>\left(w_{1}^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{2}w_{0}^{(t,m+1)},

(320)

c_{2}^{(t)}
will increase, i.e.,
c_{2}^{(t+1)}>c_{2}^{(t)}
and if

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{m}\left(w_{0}^{(t,k)}\right)^{2}w_{1}^{(t,k)}<\left(w_{1}^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{2}w_{0}^{(t,m+1)},

(321)

c_{2}^{(t)}
will decrease, i.e.,
c_{2}^{(t+1)}<c_{2}^{(t)}
. Combining (
319
) with (
312
) and (
313
), we have

\displaystyle\exp\left(\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\mu_{2}^{(t+1)}}{N}\right)\right)\frac{\exp\left(2(1-2c_{2}^{(t+1)})\mu_{2}^{(t+1)}\right)}{\exp\left((4c_{2}^{(t+1)})\mu_{2}^{(t+1)}\right)}\asymp 1,

(322)

indicating that
c_{2}^{(t+1)}\in[0.24,0.26]
.

Next, we show by contradiction that

c_{1}^{(t+1)}\geq c_{2}^{(t+1)}-\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right).

By induction hypothesis, there exists some absolute constant
C
such that for all
s\in[T_{2}^{C},t]
,

c_{1}^{(s)}-c_{2}^{(s)}\geq-\frac{C\mathsf{poly}(\log N)}{N},

where
\mathsf{poly}(\log N)
stands for some polynomial in
\log N
.
(
155
), (
154
) and (
319
) give that

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=1}^{2m}\delta z_{3,0,0}^{(t,k)}

\displaystyle=2\sum_{k=m+2}^{2m}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(w_{1}^{(t,k)}\right)^{2}w_{0}^{(t,k)}\right],

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=1}^{2m}\delta z_{3,1,1}^{(t,k)}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\sum_{k=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(w_{1}^{(t,k)}\right)^{2}w_{0}^{(t,k)}\right].

(323)

Thus if

\displaystyle c_{1}^{(t+1)}<c_{2}^{(t+1)}-\frac{C\mathsf{poly}(\log N)}{N},

(324)

then when
\eta\lesssim\frac{1}{mN}
, we have

\displaystyle c_{1}^{(t)}<c_{2}^{(t)}-\frac{C\mathsf{poly}(\log N)}{2N}.

By (
312
), (
314
) and (
B.2.9
), when
C
is large enough, we have

\displaystyle-\delta z_{3,0,0}^{(t,m+1)}\gg-\delta z_{3,1,1}^{(t,m+1)},

leading to

c_{1}^{(t+1)}-c_{2}^{(t+1)}\geq c_{1}^{(t)}-c_{2}^{(t)}\geq c_{1}^{(t+1)}-c_{2}^{(t+1)}-\frac{C\mathsf{poly}(\log N)}{N},

where the last inequality uses induction hypothesis.
The above expression contradicts (
324
), thus we have

c_{1}^{(t+1)}\geq c_{2}^{(t+1)}-\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right).

B.2.10
Proof of Lemma
18

Note that by our definitions of
w_{0}^{(k)}
,
w_{1}^{(k)}
and Lemma
11
we could rewrite

as

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{2m}\Delta_{z}^{(k)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(w_{0}^{(t,k)}\right)^{2}\right]+\sum_{k=m+1}^{2m}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(w_{1}^{(t,k)}\right)^{2}\right].

(325)

By a similar calculation as in Step 1 of the proof of Lemma

,
Lemma

(and the fact that the relations in Lemma

still hold after time

), we have for all

\displaystyle\forall k\in[m]:\quad w_{0}^{(t,k)}

(326)

\displaystyle\forall k\in[m+1,2m]:\quad w_{1}^{(t,k)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{m+1}{\exp\left(0.47\mu_{2}^{(t)}\right)+m+1}\leq\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon}{6m}}.

(327)

Plugging the above into (
