), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{2m}\Delta_{z}^{(k)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\epsilon}{3}.

B.2.11
Proof of Lemma
23

We prove this lemma by contradiction.
Assume that there exists the first time
t_{0}\leq t
such that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{m}\left(w_{0}^{(t_{0},k)}\right)^{2}w_{1}^{(t_{0},k)}<\left(1-\frac{C^{\prime}m}{N}\right)\left(w_{1}^{(t_{0},m+1)}\right)^{2}w_{0}^{(t_{0},m+1)}

(328)

for some absolute constant
C^{\prime}
.

First note that when
t=0
, we have

\displaystyle w_{0}^{(0,k)}=\begin{cases}\frac{N}{N+k},&k\in[m]\\
\frac{N+k-m-1}{N+k},&k\in\{m+1,\ldots,2m\}\end{cases},\quad w_{1}^{(0,k)}=\begin{cases}\frac{k}{N+k},&k\in[m]\\
\frac{m+1}{N+k},&k\in\{m+1,\ldots,2m\}\end{cases},

(329)

which implies that (
246
) holds when
t=0
, thus we have
t_{0}\geq 1
.

By (
155
) we have for any
t\in{\mathbb{N}}
:

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{2m}\delta z_{3,0,1}^{(t,k)}=2\sum_{k=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(w_{0}^{(t,k)}\right)^{2}w_{1}^{(t,k)}\right]-2\mathbb{E}\left[\left(w_{1}^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{2}w_{0}^{(t,m+1)}\right].

(330)

This implies that

\left|\sum_{k=1}^{2m}\delta z_{3,0,1}^{(k)}\right|\leq\frac{m}{2},

and thus by (
184b
) we have at time
t_{0}-1
,

\displaystyle V_{3,0,1}^{(t_{0}-1)}

\displaystyle=V_{3,0,1}^{(t_{0})}+\eta\sum_{k=1}^{2m}\delta z_{3,0,1}^{(t_{0},k)}\leq V_{3,0,1}^{(t_{0})}+C\frac{1}{2N}.

(331)

Similarly, by (
151
), (
153
) and (
184a
), we have

\displaystyle U_{l,i,j}^{(t_{0}-1)}

\displaystyle=U_{l,i,j}^{(t_{0})}+{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{C}{NS}\right).

(332)

Thus by the expression of
w_{0}^{(t,k)}
and
w_{1}^{(t,k)}
(c.f.(
237
), (
238
), (
239
), (
240
), (
241
) and (
242
)), we know that

\displaystyle\forall k\in[2m]:\quad w_{0}^{(t_{0}-1,k)}=\left(1\pm{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{C}{N}\right)\right)w_{0}^{(t_{0},k)},\quad w_{1}^{(t_{0}-1,k)}=\left(1\pm{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{C}{N}\right)\right)w_{1}^{(t_{0},k)},

(333)

and therefore, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{k=1}^{m}\left(w_{0}^{(t_{0}-1,k)}\right)^{2}w_{1}^{(t_{0}-1,k)}}{\left(w_{1}^{(t_{0}-1,m+1)}\right)^{2}w_{0}^{(t_{0}-1,m+1)}}\leq\left(1+{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{C}{N}\right)\right)\frac{\sum_{k=1}^{m}\left(w_{0}^{(t_{0},k)}\right)^{2}w_{1}^{(t_{0},k)}}{\left(w_{1}^{(t_{0},m+1)}\right)^{2}w_{0}^{(t_{0},m+1)}}.

(334)

By (
328
) and the above expression, we know that when
C
is small enough, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{k=1}^{m}\left(w_{0}^{(t_{0}-1,k)}\right)^{2}w_{1}^{(t_{0}-1,k)}}{\left(w_{1}^{(t_{0}-1,m+1)}\right)^{2}w_{0}^{(t_{0}-1,m+1)}}\leq 1-\frac{C^{\prime}m}{2N}.

(335)

By the above expression and (
330
) we know that

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=1}^{2m}\delta z_{3,0,1}^{(t_{0}-1,k)}\geq\frac{C^{\prime}m}{N}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(w_{1}^{(t_{0}-1,m+1)}\right)^{2}w_{0}^{(t_{0}-1,m+1)}\right].

(336)

Thus by (
184b
) we know that
V_{3,0,1}
(recall
V_{3,0,1}=-c_{2}\mu_{2}
) will increase from
t_{0}-1
to
t_{0}
.
, i.e.,

\displaystyle c_{2}^{(t_{0})}\mu_{2}^{(t_{0})}<c_{2}^{(t_{0}-1)}\mu_{2}^{(t_{0}-1)}.

(337)

Furthermore, by (
219
) we know that

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{k=1}^{2m}\delta z_{1,0,1}^{(t_{0}-1,k)}\right|\leq\frac{2m}{S}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(w_{1}^{(t_{0}-1,m+1)}\right)^{2}w_{0}^{(t_{0}-1,m+1)}\right]\leq\frac{2C^{\prime}N}{S}\left(-\sum_{k=1}^{2m}\delta z_{3,0,1}^{(t_{0}-1,k)}\right),

(338)

suggesting we could choose the absolute constant
C^{\prime}
large enough so that

\displaystyle(1+2c_{2}^{(t_{0}-1)})\mu_{2}^{(t_{0}-1)}>(1+2c_{2}^{(t_{0})})\mu_{2}^{(t_{0})}.

(339)

(
243
), (
244
) and the above expression imply that we could choose the absolute constant
C^{\prime}
large enough so that

\displaystyle\left(w_{0}^{(t_{0},k)}\right)^{2}\left(w_{1}^{(t_{0},k)}\right)

\displaystyle>\left(w_{0}^{(t_{0}-1,k)}\right)^{2}\left(w_{1}^{(t_{0}-1,k)}\right),

(340)

\displaystyle\left(w_{1}^{(t_{0},m+1)}\right)^{2}\left(w_{0}^{(t_{0},m+1)}\right)

\displaystyle<\left(w_{1}^{(t_{0}-1,m+1)}\right)^{2}\left(w_{0}^{(t_{0}-1,m+1)}\right).

(341)

Therefore, by (
328
) we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{m}\left(w_{0}^{(t_{0}-1,k)}\right)^{2}w_{1}^{(t_{0}-1,k)}<\left(1-\frac{C^{\prime}m}{N}\right)\left(w_{1}^{(t_{0}-1,m+1)}\right)^{2}w_{0}^{(t_{0}-1,m+1)}.

(342)

This contradicts with our choice that
t_{0}
is the first time that (
328
) is satisfied.

B.2.12
Proof of Lemma
19

We first define

\displaystyle r^{(k)}\coloneqq\sum_{i=1}^{N+k}\exp\left(x_{i}^{(k-1)\top}B_{1}y_{-1}^{(k-1)}+y_{i}^{(k-1)\top}B_{2}y_{-1}^{(k-1)}+z_{i}^{(k-1)\top}B_{3}z_{-1}^{(k-1)}\right),

(343)

where we let
x_{i}^{(k-1)},y_{i}^{(k-1)},z_{i}^{(k-1)}
be the
i
-th column of
X^{(k-1)},Y^{(k-1)},Z^{(k-1)}
respectively.
r^{(k)}
is the denominator of the attention score at the
k
-th reasoning step.

For any
i,j\in\{0,\ldots,S\}
, we also define

\displaystyle\delta_{1,i,j}^{(t)}\coloneqq\sum_{k=1}^{2m}\left(\delta x_{1,i,j}^{(t,k)}+\delta y_{1,i,j}^{(t,k)}\right),

(344)

and we define
\delta_{2}^{(t)},\delta_{3}^{(t)}
likewise. Then by (
184a
) we know that

\displaystyle U^{(t+1)}_{l}=U^{(t)}_{l}-\eta\delta_{l}^{(t)}.

(345)

From Lemma
3
we can see that each
\delta_{l}^{(t)}
consists of some positive terms and some negative terms. We denote the sum of all the positive terms as
\delta_{l}^{(t,+)}
and the absolute value of the sum of all the negative terms as
\delta_{l}^{(t,-)}
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\delta_{l}^{(t)}=\delta_{l}^{(t,+)}-\delta_{l}^{(t,-)}.

(346)

With the above definitions, we prove Lemma
19
by induction. In addition, we’ll also show that after
b_{2}^{(t)}>0
,
b_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{1}^{(t)}
(i.e.,
U_{3,0,1}^{(t)}
) monotonically increases.

By our initialization, Lemma
19
and the above claim hold when
t=0
.
We assume Lemma
19
and the above claim holds at time
t
(
t\in\{0,1,\cdots,T_{1}^{B}-1\}
) and prove it at time
t+1
.

Step 1: Showing (
199a
) holds at time
t+1
.

We break discussion into two cases.

Case 1:
b_{2}^{(t)}\leq 0
.
When
b_{2}^{(t)}\leq 0
, by Lemma
14
and induction hypothesis
we can compute that

\displaystyle\delta_{2,j,j}^{(t,-)}

\displaystyle\asymp-\delta x_{2,j,j}^{(t,m+1)}

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{1}{S}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\left(1-\alpha^{(t,m+1)}_{(0,j,0),(j,1)}-\alpha^{(t,m+1)}_{(\widetilde{c}_{1}(j),j,1),(j,1)}\right)^{2}\left(\alpha^{(t,m+1)}_{(0,j,0),(j,1)}+\alpha^{(t,m+1)}_{(\widetilde{c}_{1}(j),j,1),(j,1)}\right)\Bigg|n_{1}=j\Bigg]

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{N^{2}\exp\left(\left(1+b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{3}},

(347)

and

\displaystyle\delta_{2,j,j}^{(t,+)}

\displaystyle\asymp\sum_{k=2}^{m}\frac{1}{S}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\left(\alpha^{(k)}_{(c_{1}(j),j,1),(j,1)}+\alpha^{(k)}_{(c_{1}(j),c_{1}^{2}(j),0),(j,1)}+\alpha^{(k)}_{(c_{1}(j),c_{2}(c_{1}(j)),0),(j,1)}\right)\alpha^{(k)}_{(c_{1}(j),j,1),(j,1)}

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt\cdot\left(1-\alpha^{(k)}_{(p(j),j,0),(j,1)}-\alpha^{(k)}_{(\widetilde{c}_{1}(j),j,1),(j,1)}\right)\Big|n^{-1}=j\Bigg]

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{mN\exp\left(\left(2-b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,k)}\right)^{3}}

(348)

for any
k\in\{2,\cdots,m\}
,
where the second and third line is the second term of the expression of
\delta y_{2,j,j}^{(m+1)}
given in (
14
).

By induction hypothesis we also have

\displaystyle r^{(t,m+1)}

\displaystyle=\left(1+\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right)\bigg((N-1)\exp\left(b_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)+\exp\left((b_{2}^{(t)}+1-a^{(t)})\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\exp\left((-a^{(t)}-b_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)+(m-1)\exp\left(-b_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)+\exp\left((1-b_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)\bigg)

\displaystyle\asymp N\exp\left(b_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right),

(349)

where the second relation follows from (
199d
) and (
199b
), and for all
k\in\{2,\cdots,m\}
we have

\displaystyle r^{(t,k)}

\displaystyle=\left(1+\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right)\bigg((N-2)\exp\left(b_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)+\exp\left((b_{2}^{(t)}+1)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)+\exp\left((-b_{2}^{(t)}-a^{(t)})\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\exp\left((b_{2}^{(t)}-a^{(t)})\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)+(k-2)\exp\left(-b_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)+\exp\left((1-b_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)\bigg)

\displaystyle\asymp N\exp\left(b_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right).

(350)

(note that this relations holds during Phase I.1-C, even when
b_{2}^{(t)}>0
.)
Thus we have

\displaystyle r^{(t,m+1)}

\displaystyle\asymp r^{(t,k)},\quad\forall k\in\{2,\cdots,m\}.

(351)

By (
199b
) we know that
\delta x_{2,j,j}^{(t,-)}
dominates
\delta x_{2,j,j}^{(t,+)}
, and thus

\displaystyle-\delta_{2,j,j}^{(t)}\asymp\delta_{2,j,j}^{(t,-)}\asymp\frac{\exp\left(\left(1-2b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{SN}\gtrsim\frac{1}{SN},

(352)

where the second relation follows from (
B.2.12
), (
351
), and the last relation follows from (
199b
).
(
352
) indicates that (
199a
) holds at time
t+1
if
b_{2}^{(t)}\leq 0
,

Case 2:
b_{2}^{(t)}>0
.
When
b_{2}^{(t)}>0
, by Lemma
14
and induction hypothesis we have

\displaystyle-\delta_{2,j,j}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\asymp\delta_{2,j,j}^{(t,-)}

\displaystyle\asymp\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{1}{S}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\left(1-\alpha^{(k)}_{(p(j),j,0),(j,1)}-\alpha^{(k)}_{(p(j),s(j),0),(j,1)}\right)\alpha^{(k)}_{(p(j),j,0),(j,1)}

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt\cdot\left(1-\alpha^{(k)}_{(p(j),j,0),(j,1)}-\alpha^{(k)}_{(\widetilde{c}_{1}(j),j,1),(j,1)}\right)\Big|n^{-1}=j\Bigg]

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{mN^{2}\exp\left(\left(1+3b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,k)}\right)^{3}}\asymp\frac{m\exp\left(\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{SN}\gtrsim\frac{1}{SN}.

(353)

where the first and second line corresponds to the first term of
\delta y_{2,j,j}^{(k)}
given in (
14
), and
k\in\{2,\cdots,m\}
, and the last relation uses (
B.2.12
).
Therefore, (
352
) holds at time
t+1
if
b_{2}^{(t)}>0
.

Step 2: Showing (
199b
) holds at time
t+1
if
b_{2}^{(t)}>0
, then
U_{3,0,1}^{(t+1)}\geq U_{3,0,1}^{(t)}
.

We first show by contradiction that if
b_{2}^{(t+1)}\leq 0
, then we have

\exp\left((1-2b_{2}^{(t+1)})\mu_{1}^{(t+1)}\right)\lesssim\frac{N}{m}.

Suppose for contradiction that
b_{2}^{(t+1)}\leq 0
and

\displaystyle\exp\left((1-2b_{2}^{(t+1)})\mu_{1}^{(t+1)}\right)>\frac{CN}{m}.

(354)

By the induction hypothesis we have

b_{2}^{(s)}\leq 0,\quad\forall s\in\{0,1,\cdots,t\},

and there exists some absolute constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\exp\left((1-2b_{2}^{(s)})\mu_{1}^{(s)}\right)\leq\frac{CN}{m},\quad\forall s\in\{0,1,\cdots,t\}.

(355)

By (
354
), when the learning rate
\eta\lesssim\frac{1}{mN}
, we have

\displaystyle\exp\left((1-2b_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)\geq\frac{CN}{2m}.

(356)

Using the induction hypothesis and Lemma
14
, we can compute that

\displaystyle\delta_{3,0,1}^{(t,+)}

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{1}{S}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\left(1-\alpha^{(t,m+1)}_{(n_{2},j,1),(j,1)}-\alpha^{(t,m+1)}_{(n_{2},c_{1}(n_{2}),0),(j,1)}-\alpha^{(t,m+1)}_{(n_{2},c_{2}(n_{2}),0),(j,1)}\right)\alpha^{(t,m+1)}_{(n_{2},j,1),(j,1)}

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt\cdot\left(\sum_{q\in[S]}\alpha^{(t,m+1)}_{(\widetilde{p}(q),q,0),(j,1)}\right)\Big|n_{1}=j\Bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{S}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\left(1-\alpha^{(t,m+1)}_{(0,j,0),(j,1)}-\alpha^{(t,m+1)}_{(\widetilde{c}_{1}(j),j,1),(j,1)}\right)\alpha^{(t,m+1)}_{(0,j,0),(j,1)}\left(\sum_{q\in[S]}\alpha^{(t,m+1)}_{(\widetilde{p}(q),q,0),(j,1)}\right)\Big|n_{1}=j\Bigg]

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{N^{2}\exp\left(\left(1+b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{3}},

(357)

where the second and third line corresponds to the fourth term of
\delta y_{3,0,1}^{(m+1)}
given in (
14
), and the fourth line corresponds to the third term of
\delta x_{3,0,1}^{(m+1)}
given in (
14
).
By the above expression and (
356
) we have

\displaystyle\delta_{2,j,j}^{(t,-)}+2\delta_{3,0,1}^{(t,+)}

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{N^{2}\exp\left(\left(1+b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{3}}\lesssim\frac{mN}{CS\left(r^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{3}}\exp\left((2-b_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right).

(358)

By (
B.2.12
), (
351
) and the above expression we have when
C
is large enough,
\delta_{2,j,j}^{(t,-)}+2\delta_{3,0,1}^{(t,+)}
is dominated by
\delta_{2,j,j}^{(t,+)}
, leading to

\displaystyle\delta_{2,j,j}^{(t,-)}-\delta_{2,j,j}^{(t,+)}-2\left(\delta_{3,0,1}^{(t,-)}-\delta_{3,0,1}^{(t,+)}\right)\leq 0.

(359)

This implies that

\displaystyle\left(1-2b_{2}^{(t+1)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t+1)}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-2b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)},

(360)

and thus by (
355
) we have

\exp\left((1-2b_{2}^{(t+1)})\mu_{1}^{(t+1)}\right)\leq\frac{CN}{m},

which contradicts with (
354
).

We next show

b_{2}^{(t+1)}\leq\frac{1}{2}

by contradiction. If
b_{2}^{(t+1)}>\frac{1}{2}
, then when
\eta\lesssim\frac{1}{mN}
, we have

\displaystyle b_{2}^{(t)}\geq\frac{1}{2}-{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right).

(361)

We can compute by Lemma
14
and induction hypothesis that

\displaystyle\delta_{3,0,1}^{(t,-)}

\displaystyle\asymp-\sum_{k=1}^{m}\delta y_{3,0,1}^{(k)}\asymp\frac{mN\exp\left(\left(2+b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,k)}\right)^{3}}\overset{\eqref{eq:delta_2_jj_I_1_B_d2_-}}{\leq}{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\left(-\delta_{2,j,j}^{(t)}\right),

(362)

where the last step also uses (
361
).
This implies that from step
t
to step
t+1
,

\mu_{1}^{(t+1)}-\mu_{1}^{(t)}\gtrsim N\left(b_{2}^{(t+1)}\mu_{1}^{(t+1)}-b_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right),

and thus

\displaystyle b_{2}^{(t+1)}

\displaystyle\leq b_{2}^{(t)}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

(363)

This leads to a contradiction.

Lastly, if
b_{2}^{(t)}>0
, by (
362
) and (
B.2.12
) we have

\frac{\delta_{3,0,1}^{(t,-)}}{\delta_{3,0,1}^{(t,+)}}\mu_{1}^{(t)}\asymp\frac{m\exp\left(\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{N},

indicating that
-\delta_{3,0,1}^{(t)}=\delta_{3,0,1}^{(t,-)}-\delta_{3,0,1}^{(t,+)}
increases with
t
after
b_{2}^{(t)}\geq 0
. Therefore,
U_{3,0,1}^{(t+1)}\geq U_{3,0,1}^{(t)}
.

Step 3: Showing (
199c
) holds at time
t+1
.

This can be shown by following a similar calculation as in Step 3 in the proof of Lemma
15
, and we omit the details.

Step 4: Showing (
199d
) holds at time
t+1
.

If
a^{(t+1)}\geq 0
, then by our choice of
T_{1}^{C}
we know that (
199d
) holds at time
t+1
. Below we consider the case when
a^{(t+1)}<0
.
By induction hypothesis and Lemma
14
, we can compute that

\displaystyle\delta_{1,0,j}^{(t,-)}

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{1}{S}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\left(1-\alpha^{(t,m+1)}_{(0,j,0),(j,1)}-\alpha^{(t,m+1)}_{(\widetilde{c}_{1}(j),j,1),(j,1)}\right)\alpha^{(t,m+1)}_{(0,j,0),(j,1)}\left(1-\alpha^{(t,m+1)}_{(0,j,0),(j,1)}-\alpha^{(t,m+1)}_{(n_{2^{m}},j,1),(j,1)}\right)\Big|n_{1}=j\Bigg]

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{N^{2}\exp\left(\left(3b_{2}^{(t)}+1-a^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{3}}.

(364)

where the first two lines correspond to the first term of
\delta x_{1,0,j}^{(m+1)}
given in (
14
).

We could also compute that

\displaystyle\delta_{1,0,j}^{(t,+)}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\frac{1}{S}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\left(\alpha^{(t,m+1)}_{(0,j,0),(j,1)}+\alpha^{(t,m+1)}_{(0,n_{2^{m}},1),(j,1)}\right)^{2}\left(1-\alpha^{(t,m+1)}_{(0,j,0),(j,1)}-\alpha^{(t,m+1)}_{(0,n_{2^{m}},1),(j,1)}\right)\Big|n_{1}=j\Bigg]

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{N\exp\left(\left(3b_{2}^{(t)}+2-2a^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{3}}.

(365)

By comparing the above expressions and using a contradiction argument similar as in Step 2 in the proof of Lemma
20
, we can show that

\displaystyle\exp\left((1-a^{(t)})\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)\lesssim N.

(366)

Step 5: Showing
U_{1,0,j}^{(t+1)}>U_{1,0,j}^{(t)}
.

By Lemma
14
and induction hypothesis, we can compute that for all
k\in\{m+2,\cdots,m+1+2^{m}\}
,

\displaystyle\delta x_{3,0,0}^{(k)}

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{1}{S}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\left(1-\alpha^{(k)}_{(p(j),j,0),(j,0)}-\alpha^{(k)}_{(c_{1}(j),j,1),(j,0)}\right)\alpha^{(k)}_{(c_{1}(j),j,1),(j,0)}\left(\sum_{q\in[S]}\alpha^{(k)}_{(\widetilde{p}(q),q,0),(j,0)}\right)\Big|n_{-1}=j\Bigg]

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{\left(2\exp\left(2\mu_{1}\right)+N\exp\left(\mu_{1}\right)\right)\left(N\exp\left(-b_{1}\mu_{1}\right)+(m-1)\exp(b_{1}\mu_{1})\right)}{S\left(r^{(k)}\right)^{3}},

(367)

and

\displaystyle\delta y_{3,0,0}^{(k)}

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{1}{S}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\left(1-\alpha^{(k)}_{(i,c_{1}(i),0),(j,0)}-\alpha^{(k)}_{(i,c_{2}(i),0),(j,0)}-\alpha^{(k)}_{(i,j,1),(j,0)}\right)\alpha^{(k)}_{(i,j,1),(j,0)}\left(\sum_{q\in[S]}\alpha^{(k)}_{(\widetilde{p}(q),q,0),(j,0)}\right)\Big|n_{-1}=j\Bigg]

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{\left(N\exp\left(\left(1-b_{1}\right)\mu_{1}\right)+2\exp\left(\left(2-b_{1}\right)\mu_{1}\right)+\exp\left((1+b_{1})\mu_{1}\right)\right)\left(N+2\exp(\mu_{1})\right)}{S\left(r^{(k)}\right)^{3}}.

(368)

Recall that Lemma
14
gives that for all
k\in[m+1]
:

\displaystyle\delta x_{3,0,0}^{(k+m+1)}=\delta y_{3,1,0}^{(k)}=0.

(369)

Thus by the above expressions we can see that

\delta_{3,0,0}^{(t)}=\sum_{k=m+2}^{m+1+2^{m}}\delta x_{3,0,0}^{(t,k)}+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\delta y_{3,0,0}^{(t,k)}

is strictly positive, indicating that
U_{3,0,0}^{(t+1)}<U_{3,0,0}^{(t)}
.

B.2.13
Proof of Lemma
20

We follow the same notation as in the proof of Lemma
19
.
We first show (
202
) holds by induction.

From Lemma
19
we know that when
t=T_{1}^{B}
, (
202
) holds. We assume that (
202
) holds for all
s\in\{T_{1}^{B},T_{1}^{B}+2,\cdots,t\}
(
T_{1}^{B}\leq t<T_{2}^{B}
). We next show that (
202
) also holds for
t+1
.

Step 1: Showing
\mu_{1}^{(t+1)}>\mu_{1}^{(t)}
and (
202a
) holds for
t+1
.

Similar to the calculation in Step 1 of Lemma
19
, we can compute that (note
\exp(\mu_{1}^{(t)})\gtrsim N
by induction hypothesis,
\exp\left((1-2b_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)\lesssim N
by our choice of
T_{1}^{B}
)

\displaystyle\forall k\in[m]:\quad\frac{r^{(t,m+1)}}{r^{(t,k)}}\asymp\frac{N}{\exp\left(\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)},

(370)

since

\displaystyle r^{(t,m+1)}\asymp N\exp(b_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{1}^{(t)}),\quad r^{(t,k)}\asymp\exp\left((1+b_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right),\quad\forall k\in[m].

(371)

And we can compute that

\displaystyle\delta_{2,j,j}^{(t,+)}

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{mN\exp\left(\left(2-b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,k)}\right)^{3}},

(372)

\displaystyle\delta_{2,j,j}^{(t,-)}

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{N^{2}\exp\left(\left(1+b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{3}}+\frac{mN^{2}\exp\left(\left(1+3b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,k)}\right)^{3}}.

(373)

By (
370
) and the above two expressions we can see that
\delta_{2,j,j}^{(t,-)}
dominates
\delta_{2,j,j}^{(t,+)}
, and thus

\displaystyle-\delta_{2,j,j}^{(t)}=\delta_{2,j,j}^{(t,-)}\gtrsim\frac{N^{2}\exp\left(\left(1+b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{3}}\asymp\frac{\exp\left(\left(1-2b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{SN}\geq\frac{1}{SN},

(374)

where the last inequality follows from the induction hypothesis that
b_{2}^{(t)}\leq\frac{1}{2}
.
The above expression implies that (
202a
) holds for
t+1
, and
\mu_{1}^{(t+1)}>\mu_{1}^{(t)}
.

Step 2: Showing (
202b
) holds for
t+1
.

By Lemma
14
and induction hypothesis, we can compute that

\displaystyle\delta_{1,0,j}^{(t,+)}

\displaystyle\asymp\delta y_{1,0,j}^{(t,m+1)}

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{1}{S}\sum_{i\in[S]}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbbm{1}\{n_{2^{k-m}}=i\}\left(\alpha^{(t,k)}_{(0,j,0),(j,0)}+\alpha^{(t,k)}_{(0,n_{2^{m}},1),(j,0)}\right)^{2}\left(1-\alpha^{(t,k)}_{(0,j,0),(j,0)}-\alpha^{(t,k)}_{(0,n_{2^{m}},1),(j,0)}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(1-\alpha^{(t,k)}_{(i,c_{1}(i),0),(j,0)}-\alpha^{(t,k)}_{(i,c_{2}(i),0),(j,0)}-\alpha^{(t,k)}_{(i,j,1),(j,0)}\right)\left(\alpha^{(t,k)}_{(i,c_{1}(i),0),(j,0)}+\alpha^{(t,k)}_{(i,c_{2}(i),0),(j,0)}+\alpha^{(t,k)}_{(i,j,1),(j,0)}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\left(\alpha^{(t,k)}_{(0,j,0),(j,0)}+\alpha^{(t,k)}_{(0,n_{2^{m}},1),(j,0)}\right)\Bigg|n_{1}=j\Bigg]

\displaystyle\asymp\underbrace{\frac{N\exp\left(\left(3b_{2}^{(t)}+2-2a^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{3}}}_{(a)}+\underbrace{\frac{\exp\left(\left(b_{2}^{(t)}+2-a^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)\left(N+\exp\left(\left(1-a^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{3}}}_{(b)}.

(375)

And same as (
B.2.12
), here we also have

\displaystyle\delta_{1,0,j}^{(t,-)}

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{N^{2}\exp\left(\left(3b_{2}^{(t)}+1-a^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{3}}.

(376)

We show by contradiction that

\displaystyle\exp\left((1-a^{(t)})\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)\lesssim N.

(377)

By the induction hypothesis, there exists some absolute constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\exp\left((1-a^{(s)})\mu_{1}^{(s)}\right)\leq CN,\quad\forall s\in\{T_{1}^{B},T_{1}^{B}+2,\cdots,t\}.

(378)

If at time
t+1
, we can compute that

\displaystyle\exp\left((1-a^{(t)})\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)>CN,

(379)

then when
\eta\lesssim\frac{1}{mN}
, we have

\displaystyle\exp\left((1-a^{(t)})\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)>\frac{CN}{2}.

(380)

When
C
is large enough, by (
B.2.13
) and (
376
) we have the term (a) is larger than
\delta_{1,0,j}^{(t,-)}
, and thus

\displaystyle\delta_{1,0,j}^{(t)}\asymp\delta_{1,0,j}^{(t,+)}.

(381)

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle r^{(t,m+1)}\asymp\exp\left(\left(1+b_{2}^{(t)}-a^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right),\quad r^{(t,k)}\asymp\exp\left(\left(1+b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right),\quad\forall k\in[m],

(382)

and in Step 1 we have computed that

\displaystyle-\delta_{2,j,j}^{(t)}\asymp\underbrace{\frac{N^{2}\exp\left(\left(1+b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{3}}}_{(c)}+\underbrace{\frac{mN^{2}\exp\left(\left(1+3b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,k)}\right)^{3}}}_{(d)}.

(383)

By (
B.2.13
) we have that

\displaystyle(b)\gtrsim\frac{N\exp\left(\left(b_{2}^{(t)}+2-a^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{3}}\gtrsim\frac{CN^{2}\exp\left(\left(1+b_{2}^{(t)}-a^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{3}}\gtrsim C\times(c),

(384)

where the last step uses (
380
).
By (
B.2.13
), (
383
) and (
382
) we have that

\displaystyle(d)

\displaystyle=\frac{mN^{2}\exp\left(\left(1+3b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{3}}\frac{\left(r^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{3}}{\left(r^{(t,k)}\right)^{3}}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:r_ratio_I_2_B_contradiction}}{\asymp}\frac{mN^{2}\exp\left(\left(1+3b_{2}^{(t)}-3a^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{3}}=\frac{mN^{2}\exp\left(\left(1+3b_{2}^{(t)}-3a^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{3}}.

(385)

where
k\in[m]
is arbitrary.
On the other hand, by (
B.2.13
) and (
380
) we have that

\displaystyle(a)\gtrsim\frac{CN^{2}\exp\left(\left(3b_{2}^{(t)}+1-a^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{3}}\gtrsim C\times(d).

(386)

By (
384
) and (
386
) we have

\displaystyle\delta_{1,0,j}^{(t)}\gtrsim-C\delta_{2,j,j}^{(t)}.

(387)

This indicates that we could choose
C
large enough such that

\displaystyle\left(1-a^{(t+1)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t+1)}<\left(1-a^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}.

(388)

Combining this with (
378
) we have that

\displaystyle\exp\left((1-a^{(t+1)})\mu_{1}^{(t+1)}\right)\leq CN.

(389)

This contradicts with (
379
).

Step 3: Showing (
202c
) holds for
t+1
.

Same as (
362
), (
B.2.12
) given in the proof of Lemma
19
, here we also have

\displaystyle\delta_{3,0,1}^{(t,-)}

\displaystyle\asymp-\sum_{k=1}^{m}\delta y_{3,0,1}^{(k)}\asymp\frac{mN\exp\left(\left(2+b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,k)}\right)^{3}}

(390)

and

\displaystyle\delta_{3,0,1}^{(t,+)}\asymp\frac{N^{2}\exp\left(\left(1+b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{3}}\gtrsim\frac{N^{2}\exp\left(\left(1+b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,k)}\right)^{3}}

(391)

where the last line uses (
370
). Thus following the same contradiction argument as in Step 2 in the proof of Lemma
19
, we can show that
b_{2}^{(t+1)}\leq 1/2
.

On the other hand, by our choice of
T_{2}^{B}
we have that

\displaystyle r^{(t,m+1)}\gtrsim\exp\left(\left(1-b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right).

(392)

This together with (
371
), (
391
) yields

\displaystyle\delta_{3,0,1}^{(t,+)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{N^{2}\exp\left(\left(1+b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,k)}\right)^{3}}\cdot\exp\left(6b_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{N^{2}\exp\left(\left(1+7b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,k)}\right)^{3}}\lesssim\frac{N\exp\left(\left(2+7b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,k)}\right)^{3}}.

(393)

By comparing the above expression with (
390
) we have that if
b_{2}^{(t)}\leq 0
,
\delta_{3,0,1}^{(t,-)}
dominates
\delta_{3,0,1}^{(t,+)}
, and thus it can also be shown by contradiction that
b_{2}^{(t+1)}\geq 0
.

Step 4: Showing (
202d
) holds for
t+1
, and
U_{3,1,0}^{(t+1)}>U_{3,1,0}^{(t)}
.

(
202d
) can be shown by following a similar calculation as in Step 3 in the proof of Lemma
15
, and
U_{3,1,0}^{(t+1)}>U_{3,1,0}^{(t)}
can be shown by following the same calculation as in Step 5 in the proof of Lemma
19
. We omit the details here.
The induction is complete.

We now move on to show (
203
).

Step 5: Showing (
203a
) holds.

After

\displaystyle\exp\left((1-2b_{2}^{(T_{2}^{B})})\mu_{1}^{(T_{2}^{B})}\right)\asymp N,\quad\exp\left(\left(1-a^{(T_{2}^{B})}\right)\mu_{1}^{(T_{2}^{B})}\right)\asymp N,

(394)

we can compute that

\displaystyle r^{(T_{2}^{B},m+1)}\asymp\exp\left((1-b_{2}^{(T_{2}^{B})})\mu_{1}^{(T_{2}^{B})}\right),\quad r^{(T_{2}^{B},k)}\asymp\exp\left((1+b_{2}^{(T_{2}^{B})})\mu_{1}^{(T_{2}^{B})}\right),\quad\forall k\in[m],

(395)

and

\displaystyle\delta_{3,0,1}^{(t,-)}

\displaystyle\asymp-\sum_{k=1}^{m}\delta y_{3,0,1}^{(t,k)}\asymp\frac{mN\exp\left(\left(3-b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,k)}\right)^{3}}\asymp\frac{mN\exp\left(\left(3-7b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{3}},

(396)

\displaystyle\delta_{3,0,1}^{(t,+)}\asymp\delta y_{3,0,1}^{(t,m+1)}\asymp\frac{N^{2}\exp\left(\left(3-2a^{(t)}+b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{3}}.

(397)

Note that when
b_{2}^{(t)}=1/4
, the above two terms are equal in magnitude. If
\delta_{3,0,1}^{(t,-)}
(resp.
\delta_{3,0,1}^{(t,+)}
) dominates
\delta_{3,0,1}^{(t,+)}
(resp.
\delta_{3,0,1}^{(t,-)}
), then
b_{2}^{(t+1)}
will increase (resp. decrease) to near
1/4
.
Thus by contradiction similar as in Step 3 we can show that when
C_{0}
in (
201
) is large enough, there exists
s\leq T_{2}^{B}
such that

\displaystyle\forall t\in\{s,s+1,\cdots,T_{2}^{B}\}:\quad b_{2}^{(t)}=\left(\frac{1}{4}\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\right)a^{(t)}.

(398)

Step 6: Showing (
203a
) holds.

When (
394
) is satisfied, by Lemma
14

we can compute that

\displaystyle\delta_{1,0,j}^{(t,-)}

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{1}{S}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[-\left(1-\alpha^{(t,m+1)}_{(0,j,0),(j,1)}-\alpha^{(t,m+1)}_{(\widetilde{c}_{1}(j),j,1),(j,1)}\right)\alpha^{(t,m+1)}_{(0,j,0),(j,1)}\left(1-\alpha^{(t,m+1)}_{(0,j,0),(j,1)}-\alpha^{(t,m+1)}_{(0,n_{2^{m}},1),(j,1)}\right)\Big|n_{1}=j\Bigg]

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{N\exp\left(\left(b_{2}^{(t)}+2-a^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(T_{2}^{B})}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{3}}\gtrsim\delta_{1,0,j}^{(t,+)}.

(399)

In addition,

\displaystyle\delta_{1,0,j}^{(t,+)}

\displaystyle\asymp\delta y_{1,0,j}^{(t,m+1)}

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{1}{S}\sum_{i\in[S]}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbbm{1}\{n_{2^{k-m}}=i\}\left(\alpha^{(t,k)}_{(0,j,0),(j,0)}+\alpha^{(t,k)}_{(0,n_{2^{m}},1),(j,0)}\right)^{2}\left(1-\alpha^{(t,k)}_{(0,j,0),(j,0)}-\alpha^{(t,k)}_{(0,n_{2^{m}},1),(j,0)}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(1-\alpha^{(t,k)}_{(i,c_{1}(i),0),(j,0)}-\alpha^{(t,k)}_{(i,c_{2}(i),0),(j,0)}-\alpha^{(t,k)}_{(i,j,1),(j,0)}\right)\left(\alpha^{(t,k)}_{(i,c_{1}(i),0),(j,0)}+\alpha^{(t,k)}_{(i,c_{2}(i),0),(j,0)}+\alpha^{(t,k)}_{(i,j,1),(j,0)}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\left(\alpha^{(t,k)}_{(0,j,0),(j,0)}+\alpha^{(t,k)}_{(0,n_{2^{m}},1),(j,0)}\right)\Bigg|n_{1}=j\Bigg]

\displaystyle\asymp\underbrace{\frac{N\exp\left(\left(3b_{2}^{(t)}+2-2a^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{3}}}_{(a)}+\underbrace{\frac{\exp\left(\left(b_{2}^{(t)}+2-a^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)\left(N+\exp\left(\left(1-a^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{3}}}_{(b)}.

(400)

By comparing the above two expressions we can see that if

\left(1-a^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\lesssim CN

for some small enough constant
C>0
, then
\delta_{1,0,j}^{(t,-)}
dominates
\delta_{1,0,j}^{(t,+)}
, causing
a^{(t+1)}\mu_{1}^{(t+1)}<a^{(t)}\mu_{1}^{(t)}
. And since
\mu_{1}^{(t+1)}>\mu_{1}^{(t)}
,
(1-a^{(t+1)})\mu_{1}^{(t+1)}
increases faster than
\mu_{1}^{(t)}
and could reacch
{\mathcal{O}}(N)
before
t
reaches
T_{2}^{B}
.

B.2.14
Proof of Lemma
21

Proof of (
206
).

We use the same notation as in the proof of Lemma
19
, and prove (
206
) by induction.
By Lemma
20
we know that that (
206
) holds for
t=T_{2}^{B}
. We assume that (
206
) holds for
s\in\{T_{2}^{B},T_{2}^{B}+1,\cdots,t\}
, and will show that it continues to hold for
t+1
.
In fact, the relations (
206b
), (
206c
) and (
206d
) hold for
t+1
, as well as that
b_{1}^{(t+1)}\mu_{1}^{(t+1)}>b_{1}^{(t)}\mu_{1}^{(t)}
can be shown by following a similar calculation as in the proof of Lemma
20
. We omit the details here and only compute that (
206a
) holds for
t+1
.

Similar as in the proof of Lemma
20
, we can compute that

\displaystyle r^{(t,k)}=\begin{cases}\exp\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\pm\frac{\mu_{1}^{(t)}}{N}\right)\right)\exp\left((1+b_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right),&k\in[m],\\
\exp\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\pm\frac{\mu_{1}^{(t)}}{N}\right)\right)\exp\left((1-b_{2}^{(t)})\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right),&k=m+1,\\
\exp\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\pm\frac{\mu_{1}^{(t)}}{N}\right)\right)\exp\left((1+b_{1}^{(t)})\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right),&k\in\{m+2,\cdots,2m\}.\end{cases}.

(401)

Then following a similar calculation as in the proof of Lemma
20
, we can compute that

\displaystyle\delta_{2,j,j}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\mu_{1}^{(t)}}{N}\right)\right)\frac{N^{2}\exp\left(\left(1+b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,m+1)}\right)^{3}}

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\mu_{1}^{(t)}}{N}\right)\right)\frac{N^{2}\exp\left(\left(4b_{2}^{(t)}-2\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\exp\left(\left(4\times 0.24-2-0.01\right)\mu_{1}^{(T_{3}^{B})}\right)}{S}\overset{\eqref{eq:T_3_B}}{\geq}\frac{1}{S}\left(\frac{\epsilon}{6m}\right)^{3/2}

(402)

when
\epsilon
is small enough, where the last step uses the induction hypothesis, and thus (
206a
) holds for
t+1
.

Proof of (
207
).

By (
206b
) we know that when
\epsilon
is small enough, (
207a
) holds.
For (
207b
), by computation we could verify that
(
B.2.12
), (
B.2.12
) is still valid here, and by which we deduce that when
\mu_{1}^{(t)}
is large enough:

\displaystyle\delta_{3,0,0}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\mu_{1}^{(t)}}{N}\right)\right)\Bigg(\sum_{k=m+1}^{2m}\frac{\left(2\exp\left(2\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)+N\exp\left(\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)\right)\left(N\exp\left(-b_{1}^{(t)}\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)+(m-1)\exp(b_{1}^{(t)}\mu_{1}^{(t)})\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,k)}\right)^{3}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\frac{\left(N\exp\left(\left(1-b_{1}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)+2\exp\left(\left(2-b_{1}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)+\exp\left((1+b_{1}^{(t)})\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)\right)\left(N+2\exp(\mu_{1}^{(t)})\right)}{S\left(r^{(t,k)}\right)^{3}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:r_II_B}}{=}\exp\left(\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\mu_{1}^{(t)}}{N}\right)\right)\frac{\exp\left(\left(3-b_{1}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)+\exp\left(\left(2+b_{1}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\left(\left(3+3b_{1}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)^{3}}.

(403)

On the other hand, we can verify by straightforward calculation that (
396
) and (
397
) are still valid, by which we have

\displaystyle-\delta_{3,0,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\exp\left(\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\mu_{1}^{(t)}}{N}\right)\right)\frac{\exp\left(\left(3-b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{S\exp\left(\left(3+3b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}.

(404)

By comparing the above two expressions and
using contradiction similar as, for example, Step 2 in the proof of Lemma
20
, we can show that

b_{1}^{(t)}\geq b_{2}^{(t)}-\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right).

B.2.15
Proof of Lemma
22

By Lemma
11
we know that
for all
k\in[m]
, we have

\displaystyle\Delta_{x}^{(t,k)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{S}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbbm{1}\{n_{-1}=j\}\Bigg\{\left(1-\alpha^{(t,k)}_{(p(j),j,0),(j,1)}-\alpha^{(t,k)}_{(\widetilde{c}_{1}(j),j,1),(j,1)}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\sum_{q\in[S]\setminus\{j\}}\left(\alpha^{(t,k)}_{(p(q),q,0),(j,1)}+\alpha^{(t,k)}_{(\widetilde{c}_{1}(q),q,1),(j,1)}\right)^{2}\Bigg\}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{S}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbbm{1}\{n_{-1}=j\}\Bigg\{\left(1-\alpha^{(t,k)}_{(p(j),j,0),(j,1)}-\alpha^{(t,k)}_{(\widetilde{c}_{1}(j),j,1),(j,1)}\right)^{2}\Bigg\}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\mu_{1}^{(t)}}{N}\right)\right)\left(\frac{(N+2m-1)\exp\left(b_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{\exp\left(\left(1+b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)+(N+2m-1)\exp\left(b_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}\right)^{2}.

(405)

where the second inequality follows from that fact that

(u_{1}+\ldots+u_{n})^{2}\geq\sum_{i=1}^{n}u_{i}^{2},\quad\forall u_{i}\geq 0,

and the last relation uses Lemma
21
(and the fact that the relations other than (
206a
) in Lemma
21
still hold after time
T_{3}^{B}
).

Following the same computation we can obtain that when
k=m+1
,

\displaystyle\Delta_{x}^{(t,m+1)}

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\mu_{1}^{(t)}}{N}\right)\right)\left(\frac{(N+2m-1)\exp\left(b_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{\exp\left(\left(1-b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)+(N+2m-1)\exp\left(b_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}\right)^{2},

(406)

when
k\in\{m+2,\cdots,2m\}
,

\displaystyle\Delta_{x}^{(t,k)}

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\mu_{1}^{(t)}}{N}\right)\right)\left(\frac{(N+2m-1)\exp\left(b_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{\exp\left(\left(1+b_{1}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)+(N+2m-1)\exp\left(b_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}\right)^{2},

(407)

and for
\Delta_{y}^{(t,k)}
, we have for all
k\in[m]
,

\displaystyle\Delta_{y}^{(t,k)}

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\mu_{1}^{(t)}}{N}\right)\right)\left(\frac{(N+2m-1)\exp\left(1-b_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{\exp\left(\left(1+b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)+(N+2m-1)\exp\left(1-b_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}\right)^{2},

(408)

\displaystyle\Delta_{y}^{(t,m+1)}

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\mu_{1}^{(t)}}{N}\right)\right)\left(\frac{(N+2m-1)\exp\left(1-b_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{\exp\left(\left(1+b_{2}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)+(N+2m-1)\exp\left(1-b_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}\right)^{2},

(409)

and for
k\in\{m+2,\cdots,2m\}
,

\displaystyle\Delta_{y}^{(t,k)}

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\mu_{1}^{(t)}}{N}\right)\right)\left(\frac{(N+2m-1)\exp\left(\max\{1-b_{1}^{(t)},b_{1}^{(t)}\}\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{\exp\left(\left(1+b_{1}^{(t)}\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)+(N+2m-1)\exp\left(\max\{1-b_{1}^{(t)},b_{1}^{(t)}\}\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}\right)^{2},

(410)

and by Lemma
21
, all
\Delta_{\xi}^{(t,k)}
(
\xi\in\{x,y\}
,
k\in[2m]
) can be upper bounded by the following:

\displaystyle\Delta_{\xi}^{(t,k)}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{(N+2m-1)\exp\left(1-b_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}{\exp\left(\left(1+b_{2}^{(t)}-0.01\right)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)+(N+2m-1)\exp\left(1-b_{2}^{(t)}\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{N+2m-1}{\exp\left((0.24\times 2\times 0.99-0.01)\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)+N+2m-1}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{N+2m-1}{\exp\left(0.46\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)+N+2m-1}\right)^{2}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{6m},

(411)

as long as
\epsilon
is small enough, where the last inequality follows from (
205
).
This gives (
209
).

Appendix C
Generalization Analysis

C.1
Proof of Theorem
4

Notation.

Throughout the proof, we suppose
Assumptions
3
and
4
hold, and the model is trained for
t\geq T
steps under the training distribution, where
T
is the same as in Theorem
3
. Let

E^{(k-1)}=(E,\widehat{O}^{(k-1)})

be the input at the
k
-th reasoning step. Define

\displaystyle x_{i}\coloneqq E^{(\widetilde{m})}(1:d_{1},i),\quad y_{i}\coloneqq E^{(\widetilde{m})}(d_{1}+1:2d_{1},i),\quad\forall i\in[\widetilde{N}+\widetilde{m}+1],

and

\displaystyle\widehat{x}^{(k)}\coloneqq\widehat{o}^{(k)}(1:d_{1}),\quad\widehat{y}^{(k)}\coloneqq\widehat{o}^{(k)}(d_{1}+1:2d_{1}),

\displaystyle x^{(k)}\coloneqq o^{(k)}(1:d_{1}),\quad y^{(k)}\coloneqq o^{(k)}(d_{1}+1:2d_{1}).

Furthermore, let
r=r({\mathcal{T}})
and
g=g({\mathcal{T}})
denote the root and goal node of
{\mathcal{T}}
, respectively. Let
p(i)
be the parent of
i
in
{\mathcal{T}}
, with
p(r)=0
. Let
c(i)=c(i;{\mathcal{T}})
be the child set of
i
, with
c(0)=\{r\}
.
We also let

\displaystyle\mu^{(t)}\coloneqq H_{1,1}^{(t)},\quad\nu^{(t)}\coloneqq H_{1,2}^{(t)}.

(412)

Then for all
i,j\in[S]
,
i\neq j
, we have
H_{j,j}^{(t)}=\mu^{(t)}
and
H_{i,j}^{(t)}=\nu^{(t)}
.

We first give the following lemma, whose proof is provided in Appendix
C.1.1
.

Lemma 24
.

For any
q\in[\widetilde{N}+1+\widetilde{m}]
, there exists
\{\beta_{i}^{(q)}\}_{i=1}^{S}
such that

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}x_{q}\\
y_{q}\end{pmatrix}=\sum_{i=0}^{S}\beta_{i}^{(q)}\begin{pmatrix}a_{p(i)}\\
a_{i}\end{pmatrix},\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{i=0}^{S}\beta_{i}^{(q)}=1,\,\,\beta_{i}^{(q)}\geq 0,\,\,\forall 0\leq i\leq S.

(413)

Main proof.

We begin with reformulating the test-time loss.
Define

\displaystyle\alpha^{(1)}=\frac{\exp(\mu)}{\exp(\mu)+\left(\widetilde{N}-1\right)\exp(\nu)+1}=\beta_{g}^{(\widetilde{N}+2)},

(414)

which is the attention weight of
x_{-1}^{(0)}=y^{(1)}=a_{g}
on
a_{g}
. Further, define

\displaystyle\forall k\in[\widetilde{m}]:\quad\alpha^{(k)}\coloneqq\beta_{p^{k-1}(g)}^{(\widetilde{N}+1+k)}.

(415)

Then
\alpha^{(k)}
is the proportion of
o^{(k)}
in
\widehat{o}^{(k)}
.
By our definition, we have for all
k\in[\widetilde{m}]
,

\displaystyle\widehat{o}^{(k)}=\alpha^{(k)}o^{(k)}+\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{V}}({\mathcal{T}})\atop i\neq p^{k-1}(g)}\beta_{i}^{(k)}\begin{pmatrix}a_{p(i)}\\
a_{i}\end{pmatrix},\quad o^{(k)}=\begin{pmatrix}a_{p^{k}(g)}\\
a_{p^{k-1}(g)}\end{pmatrix},

(416)

and

\displaystyle\alpha^{(k)}+\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{V}}({\mathcal{T}})\atop i\neq p^{k-1}(g)}\beta_{i}^{(k)}=1.

(417)

Thus we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathsf{test}}({\mathcal{T}};\theta^{(t)})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{\widetilde{m}}\left\|\widehat{o}^{(k)}-o^{(k)}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{\widetilde{m}}\left\|\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{V}}({\mathcal{T}})\atop i\neq p^{k-1}(g)}\beta_{i}^{(k)}\begin{pmatrix}a_{p(i)}\\
a_{i}\end{pmatrix}-\left(1-\alpha^{(k)}\right)\begin{pmatrix}a_{p^{k}(g)}\\
a_{p^{k-1}(g)}\end{pmatrix}\right\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{\widetilde{m}}\left\|\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{V}}({\mathcal{T}})\atop i\neq p^{k-1}(g)}\beta_{i}^{(k)}a_{p(i)}-\left(1-\alpha^{(k)}\right)a_{p^{k}(g)}\right\|^{2}_{2}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{\widetilde{m}}\left\|\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{V}}({\mathcal{T}})\atop i\neq p^{k-1}(g)}\beta_{i}^{(k)}a_{i}-\left(1-\alpha^{(k)}\right)a_{p^{k-1}(g)}\right\|^{2}_{2}.

(418)

The first term can be bounded via

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{V}}({\mathcal{T}})\atop i\neq p^{k-1}(g)}\beta_{i}^{(k)}a_{p(i)}-\left(1-\alpha^{(k)}\right)a_{p^{k}(g)}\right\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{V}}({\mathcal{T}})\atop i\neq p^{k-1}(g)}\left(\beta_{i}^{(k)}\right)^{2}+\left(1-\alpha^{(k)}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\max_{i\in{\mathcal{V}}({\mathcal{T}})\atop i\neq p^{k-1}(g)}|\beta_{i}^{(k)}|\cdot\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{V}}({\mathcal{T}})\atop i\neq p^{k-1}(g)}\beta_{i}^{(k)}+\left(1-\alpha^{(k)}\right)^{2}\overset{\eqref{eq:coefficient_q_sum=1}}{\leq}2(1-\alpha^{(k)})^{2}.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{V}}({\mathcal{T}})\atop i\neq p^{k-1}(g)}\beta_{i}^{(k)}a_{i}-\left(1-\alpha^{(k)}\right)a_{p^{k-1}(g)}\right\|^{2}_{2}\leq 2(1-\alpha^{(k)})^{2}.

Substituting the above two inequalities into (
C.1
), we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathsf{test}}({\mathcal{T}};\theta^{(t)})

\displaystyle\leq 2\widetilde{m}(\underbrace{1-\alpha^{(k)}}_{\coloneqq\delta^{(k)}})^{2}.

(419)

Therefore, we can bound the test-time loss by bounding

\displaystyle\delta^{(k)}\coloneqq 1-\alpha^{(k)}.

(420)

We give the following lemma, where
T_{2}
is defined in (
65
). The proof is deferred to Appendix
C.1.2
.

Lemma 25

(bounding
\delta^{(k)}
)
.

For all
k\in[\widetilde{m}]
, we have

\displaystyle\delta^{(k)}\leq\max\left\{1,\frac{\widetilde{N}+\widetilde{m}-1}{N+m-2}\right\}\cdot 2\delta,

(421)

where

\displaystyle\delta\coloneqq 1-\frac{\exp(\mu^{(t)})}{(N+m-2)\exp(\nu^{(t)})+\exp(\mu^{(t)})+1}\overset{\eqref{eq:T_2}}{\leq}\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon}{2m}}

(422)

for
t>T_{2}
.

By Lemma
25
and (
419
), we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathsf{test}}({\mathcal{T}};\theta^{(t)})

\displaystyle\leq\max\left\{1,\left(\frac{\widetilde{N}+\widetilde{m}-1}{N+m-2}\right)^{2}\right\}\cdot 4\frac{\widetilde{m}}{m}\epsilon.

(423)

C.1.1
Proof of Lemma
24

We prove this lemma by induction on
k
. First, (
413
) holds trivially for
1\leq q\leq\widetilde{N}+1
(
(x_{q},y_{q})^{\top}
is in
E^{(0)}
). Assume that (
413
) holds for
q\leq s-1
(
\widetilde{N}+2\leq s\leq\widetilde{N}+1+\widetilde{m}
). We prove that (
413
) holds for
s
.

For notational convenience, we let
k=s-1-\widetilde{N}
,
and let

\displaystyle\gamma^{(k)}=(\gamma_{1}^{(k)},\ldots,\gamma_{s-1}^{(k)})=\mathsf{softmax}\left(Y^{(k-1)\top}B\widehat{x}^{(k-1)}\right).

(424)

Then by (
5
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}x_{s}\\
y_{s}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\widehat{x}^{(k)}\\
\widehat{y}^{(k)}\end{pmatrix}=\sum_{i=1}^{s-1}\gamma_{i}^{(k)}\begin{pmatrix}x_{i}\\
y_{i}\end{pmatrix}=\sum_{j=0}^{S}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{s-1}\gamma_{i}^{(k)}\beta_{j}^{(i)}\right)\begin{pmatrix}a_{p(j)}\\
a_{j}\end{pmatrix},

where the second equality uses the induction hypothesis.
Let

\beta_{j}^{(s)}=\sum_{i=1}^{s-1}\gamma_{i}^{(k)}\beta_{j}^{(i)}
,
then we have

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}x_{s}\\
y_{s}\end{pmatrix}=\sum_{j=0}^{S}\beta_{j}^{(s)}\begin{pmatrix}a_{p(j)}\\
a_{j}\end{pmatrix},

(425)

and by the induction hypothesis, we have
\beta_{j}^{(s)}\geq 0
,
\forall j\in[S]
and

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{S}\beta_{j}^{(s)}=\sum_{j=0}^{S}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{s-1}\gamma_{i}^{(k)}\right)\beta_{j}^{(i)}\overset{\eqref{eq:gamma_k}}{=}\sum_{j=0}^{S}\beta_{j}^{(i)}=1.

(426)

This completes the induction.

C.1.2
Proof of Lemma
25

We prove by induction.
When
k=1
, by (
422
) and (
414
) we immediately know that if
N+m-2\geq\widetilde{N}-1
, we have
\delta^{(1)}\leq\delta
. If
N+m-2<\widetilde{N}-1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\delta^{(1)}}{\delta}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{\left(\widetilde{N}-1\right)\exp(\nu^{(t)})+1}{\left(N+m-2\right)\exp(\nu^{(t)})+1}}_{\leq\frac{\widetilde{N}-1}{N+m-2}}\cdot\underbrace{\frac{\exp(\mu^{(t)})+\left(N+m-2\right)\exp(\nu^{(t)})+1}{\exp(\mu^{(t)})+\left(\widetilde{N}-1\right)\exp(\nu^{(t)})+1}}_{\leq 1}\leq\frac{\widetilde{N}-1}{N+m-2}.

(427)

Thus in either case, we have
(
421
) holds for
k=1
.

Assume that (
421
) holds for all
s\in[k]
(
1\leq k\leq\widetilde{m}-1
). We aim to show that (
421
) holds for
k+1
.
Let
B=B^{(t)}
, where
t\geq T_{2}
. Then
a_{p^{k}(g)}=x^{(k)}=y^{(k+1)}
. And note that
\beta_{r}^{(k)}
is the proportion of
(0,a_{r})^{\top}
in
\widehat{o}^{(k)}
, thus we have

\displaystyle a_{p^{k}(g)}^{\top}B\widehat{x}^{(k)}=\alpha^{(k)}\mu+(1-\alpha^{(k)}-\beta_{r}^{(k)})\nu.

(428)

For all
i\in{\mathcal{V}}({\mathcal{T}})\setminus\{p^{k}(g)\}
, we have

\displaystyle a_{i}^{\top}B\widehat{x}^{(k)}\leq(1-\alpha^{(k)}-\beta_{r}^{(k)})\mu+\alpha^{(k)}\nu.

(429)

We define
p^{-1}(g)=0
for all
s\in[k]
, and recall
c(0)=\{r\}
, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{y}^{(s)\top}B\widehat{x}^{(k)}

\displaystyle=\left(\alpha^{(s)}a_{p^{s-1}(g)}+\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{V}}({\mathcal{T}})\cup\{0\}\atop i\neq p^{s-1}(g)}\beta_{i}^{(s)}a_{i}\right)^{\top}B\left(\alpha^{(k)}a_{p^{k}(g)}+\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{V}}({\mathcal{T}})\cup\{0\}\atop i\neq p^{k-1}(g)}\beta_{i}^{(i)}a_{p(i)}\right)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\bigg(\alpha^{(s)}\beta_{p^{s-2}}+\beta^{(s)}_{p^{k}(g)}\alpha^{(k)}+\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{V}}({\mathcal{T}})\cup\{0\}\atop i\neq p^{s-1}(g),p^{k}(g)}\beta_{i}^{(s)}\sum_{j\in c(i)}\beta_{j}^{(k)}\bigg)}_{\triangle}\mu

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg((1-\beta_{r}^{(k)})(1-\beta_{0}^{(s)})-\underbrace{\bigg(\alpha^{(s)}\beta_{p^{s-2}}+\beta^{(s)}_{p^{k}(g)}\alpha^{(k)}+\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{V}}({\mathcal{T}})\cup\{0\}\atop i\neq p^{s-1}(g),p^{k}(g)}\beta_{i}^{(s)}\sum_{j\in c(i)}\beta_{j}^{(k)}\bigg)}_{\triangle}\bigg)\nu.

(430)

Note that

\displaystyle\triangle

\displaystyle\leq\alpha^{(s)}(1-\alpha^{(k)}-\beta_{r}^{(k)})+\alpha^{(k)}(1-\alpha^{(s)}-\beta_{0}^{(s)})+(1-\alpha^{(s)}-\beta_{0}^{(s)})(1-\alpha^{(k)}-\beta_{r}^{(k)})

\displaystyle=(1-\beta_{r}^{(k)})(1-\beta_{0}^{(s)})-\alpha^{(s)}\alpha^{(k)},

(431)

and by Theorem
3
, we have
\mu>\nu
and
\mu>0
. Thus we have

\displaystyle\widehat{y}^{(s)\top}B\widehat{x}^{(k)}

\displaystyle\leq\left((1-\beta_{r}^{(k)})(1-\beta_{0}^{(s)})-\alpha^{(s)}\alpha^{(k)}\right)\mu+\alpha^{(s)}\alpha^{(k)}\nu

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\beta_{r}^{(k)}-\alpha^{(s)}\alpha^{(k)}\right)\mu+\alpha^{(s)}\alpha^{(k)}\nu.

(432)

Combining (
428
), (
429
), and (
C.1.2
), and denote
\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}=\min_{s\in[k]}\{\alpha^{(s)}\}
, we have

\displaystyle\alpha^{(k+1)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\exp\left(a_{p^{k}(g)}^{\top}B\widehat{x}^{(k)}\right)}{\exp\left(a_{p^{k}(g)}^{\top}B\widehat{x}^{(k)}\right)+\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{V}}({\mathcal{T}})\atop i\neq p^{k}(g)}\exp\left(a_{i}^{\top}B\widehat{x}^{(k)}\right)+\sum_{s=1}^{k}\exp\left(\widehat{y}^{(s)\top}B\widehat{x}^{(k)}\right)+1}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\exp\left(\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}\mu+(1-\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}-\beta_{r}^{(k)})\nu\right)}{\exp\left(\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}\mu+(1-\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}-\beta_{r}^{(k)})\nu\right)+(\widetilde{N}-2+\widetilde{m})\exp\left(\left(1-\beta_{r}^{(k)}-\left(\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}\right)^{2}\right)\mu+\left(\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}\right)^{2}\nu\right)+1}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\exp\left(\left(\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}+\left(\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}\right)^{2}-1+\beta_{r}^{(k)}\right)(\mu-\nu)\right)}{\exp\left(\left(\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}+\left(\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}\right)^{2}-1+\beta_{r}^{(k)}\right)(\mu-\nu)\right)+\widetilde{N}-2+\widetilde{m}+\exp\left(-\left(\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}\right)^{2}\nu\right)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\exp\left(\left(\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}+\left(\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}\right)^{2}-1\right)(\mu-\nu)\right)}{\exp\left(\left(\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}+\left(\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}\right)^{2}-1\right)(\mu-\nu)\right)+\widetilde{N}-2+\widetilde{m}+\exp\left(-\left(\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}\right)^{2}\nu\right)},

(433)

where the second line uses the following fact:

\displaystyle(1-\alpha^{(k)}-\beta_{r}^{(k)})\mu+\alpha^{(k)}\nu

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\beta_{r}^{(k)}-\alpha^{(s)}\alpha^{(k)}\right)\mu+\alpha^{(s)}\alpha^{(k)}\nu

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\beta_{r}^{(k)}-\left(\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}\right)^{2}\right)\mu+\left(\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}\right)^{2}\nu.

(434)

Define

\widetilde{\delta}^{(k)}=1-\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}=\max_{s\in[k]}\{\delta^{(s)}\},

then we have

\displaystyle 1-\left(\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}+\left(\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}\right)^{2}-1\right)=\left(2+\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}\right)\left(1-\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}\right)\leq 3\widetilde{\delta}^{(k)}.

(435)

Combining the above inequality with (
C.1.2
), we have

\displaystyle\alpha^{(k+1)}\geq\frac{\exp\left(\left(1-3\widetilde{\delta}^{(k)}\right)(\mu-\nu)\right)}{\exp\left(\left(1-3\widetilde{\delta}^{(k)}\right)(\mu-\nu)\right)+\widetilde{N}-2+\widetilde{m}+\exp\left(-\left(\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}\right)^{2}\nu\right)},

(436)

which gives

\displaystyle\delta^{(k+1)}=1-\alpha^{(k+1)}\leq\frac{\widetilde{N}-2+\widetilde{m}+\exp\left(-\left(\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}\right)^{2}\nu\right)}{\exp\left(\left(1-3\widetilde{\delta}^{(k)}\right)(\mu-\nu)\right)+\widetilde{N}-2+\widetilde{m}+\exp\left(-\left(\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}\right)^{2}\nu\right)}.

(437)

On the other hand, (
422
) gives

\displaystyle\mu-\nu=\log\left((N+m-2+\exp(-\nu))\left(\frac{1}{\delta}-1\right)\right).

(438)

Thus if
\exp(-\nu)\leq\widetilde{N}-2+\widetilde{m}
, we have

\displaystyle\mu-\nu\leq\log\left(2(N+m-2)\left(\frac{1}{\delta}-1\right)\right).

(439)

If
\exp(-\nu)\geq\widetilde{N}-2+\widetilde{m}
, we have

\displaystyle\mu-\nu\leq\log\left(2\exp(-\nu)\left(\frac{1}{\delta}-1\right)\right)\leq\log\left(2\left(\frac{1}{\delta}-1\right)\right)-\nu.

(440)

By (
74
) and Lemma
10
, we know that there exists
\epsilon_{1}(m)>0
such that for all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{1}(m)]
, after
t>T_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle-\nu\leq\frac{5}{S}\mu\leq\frac{5}{S}(\mu-\nu),

(441)

where the second inequality uses the fact that
\nu\leq 0
, since
\exp(-\nu)\geq\widetilde{N}-2+\widetilde{m}
. Combining the above two inequalities, we have

\displaystyle\mu-\nu\leq 2\log\left(2\left(\frac{1}{\delta}-1\right)\right).

(442)

Combining (
439
) and (
442
), we have when
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{1}(m)]
,

\displaystyle\mu-\nu\leq 2\log\left(2(N+m-2)\left(\frac{1}{\delta}-1\right)\right).

(443)

Note that

\displaystyle\delta\log\left(2(N+m-2)\left(\frac{1}{\delta}-1\right)\right)\rightarrow 0\quad\text{as }\delta\rightarrow 0+.

(444)

Thus we could choose
\epsilon_{0}(m,\widetilde{N},\widetilde{m})\leq\epsilon_{1}(m)
small enough such that for all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0}(m,\widetilde{N},\widetilde{m})]
, we have

\displaystyle 6\frac{\widetilde{N}+\widetilde{m}-1}{N+m-2}\delta(\mu-\nu)\leq\log 2,

(445)

which gives

\displaystyle\exp\left(3\overline{\delta}^{(k)}(\mu-\nu)\right)\leq\exp\left(6\max\left\{1,\frac{\widetilde{N}+\widetilde{m}-1}{N+m-2}\right\}\delta(\mu-\nu)\right)\leq 2,

(446)

where the first inequality uses induction hypothesis.

Define

\displaystyle a\coloneqq 2\max\left\{1,\frac{\widetilde{N}+\widetilde{m}-1}{N+m-2}\right\}.

(447)

Then

•

if
\widetilde{N}+\widetilde{m}-2<N+m-2
, we have
a=2

and

\displaystyle\frac{N+m-2+\exp(-\nu)}{\widetilde{N}+\widetilde{m}-2+\exp\left(-\left(\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}\right)^{2}\nu\right)}\geq\begin{cases}\frac{N+m-2}{\widetilde{N}+\widetilde{m}-1}\geq 1\qquad\mbox{if}\;\nu\geq 0\\
\frac{N+m-2+\exp(-\nu)}{\widetilde{N}+\widetilde{m}-2+\exp\left(-\nu\right)}\geq 1\qquad\mbox{if}\;\nu<0\end{cases},

(448)

which gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{a\delta}-1=\frac{1}{2\delta}-1\leq\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{1}{\delta}-1\right)\leq\frac{\left(\frac{1}{\delta}-1\right)(N+m-2+\exp(-\nu))}{2\left(\widetilde{N}+\widetilde{m}-2+\exp\left(-\left(\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}\right)^{2}\nu\right)\right)}.

(449)

•

if
\widetilde{N}+\widetilde{m}-2\geq N+m-2
, we have

a=2\cdot\frac{\widetilde{N}+\widetilde{m}-1}{N+m-2}\geq 1
,
and

\displaystyle\frac{2}{a}\left(\frac{1}{\delta}-a\right)=\frac{N+m-2}{\widetilde{N}+\widetilde{m}-1}\left(\frac{1}{\delta}-a\right)\leq\frac{N+m-2}{\widetilde{N}+\widetilde{m}-1}\left(\frac{1}{\delta}-1\right).

(450)

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\frac{N+m-2+\exp(-\nu)}{\widetilde{N}+\widetilde{m}-2+\exp\left(-\left(\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}\right)^{2}\nu\right)}\geq\begin{cases}\frac{N+m-2+\exp(-\nu)}{\widetilde{N}+\widetilde{m}-2+\exp\left(-\nu\right)}\geq\frac{N+m-2}{\widetilde{N}+\widetilde{m}-1}\qquad\mbox{if}\;\nu<0\\
\frac{N+m-2}{\widetilde{N}+\widetilde{m}-1}\qquad\mbox{if}\;\nu\geq 0.\end{cases}

(451)

Combined with (
450
), we have when
\widetilde{N}+\widetilde{m}-2\geq N+m-2
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{a\delta}-1\leq\frac{1}{2}\cdot\frac{N+m-2}{\widetilde{N}+\widetilde{m}-1}\left(\frac{1}{\delta}-1\right)\leq\frac{\left(\frac{1}{\delta}-1\right)(N+m-2+\exp(-\nu))}{2\left(\widetilde{N}+\widetilde{m}-2+\exp\left(-\left(\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}\right)^{2}\nu\right)\right)}.

(452)

By (
449
) and (
452
), we have the above relation holds for both cases. Furthermore, from this relation we derive that

\displaystyle\exp(\mu-\nu)

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:mu-nu}}{=}(N+m-2+\exp(-\nu))\left(\frac{1}{\delta}-1\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:case_II}}{\geq}2\left(\widetilde{N}+\widetilde{m}-2+\exp\left(-\left(\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}\right)^{2}\nu\right)\right)\left(\frac{1}{a\delta}-1\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:lowerbound_delta_mu-nu}}{\geq}\exp\left(3\overline{\delta}^{(k)}(\mu-\nu)\right)\left(\widetilde{N}+\widetilde{m}-2+\exp\left(-\left(\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}\right)^{2}\nu\right)\right)\left(\frac{1}{a\delta}-1\right).

(453)

Thus we have

\displaystyle\exp\left((1-3\overline{\delta}^{(k)})(\mu-\nu)\right)\geq\left(\widetilde{N}+\widetilde{m}-2+\exp\left(-\left(\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}\right)^{2}\nu\right)\right)\left(\frac{1}{a\delta}-1\right)

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\quad\frac{\exp\left((1-3\overline{\delta}^{(k)})(\mu-\nu)\right)}{\widetilde{N}+\widetilde{m}-2+\exp\left(-\left(\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}\right)^{2}\nu\right)}\geq\frac{1}{a\delta}-1

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\quad\frac{\widetilde{N}+\widetilde{m}-2+\exp\left(-\left(\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}\right)^{2}\nu\right)}{\widetilde{N}+\widetilde{m}-2+\exp\left(-\left(\widetilde{\alpha}^{(k)}\right)^{2}\nu\right)+\exp\left((1-3\overline{\delta}^{(k)})(\mu-\nu)\right)}\leq a\delta.

(454)

Combining the above with (
437
), we have

\displaystyle\delta^{(k+1)}\leq a\delta.

(455)

The induction is complete.

C.2
Proof of Theorem
6

Assume the tree
{\mathcal{T}}
at test time is
{\mathcal{T}}
with
\widetilde{N}
distinct nodes chosen from
[S]
and a path length
\widetilde{m}
, and Assumption
3
,
4
hold, and the model is trained for
t\geq\max\{T_{3}^{B},T_{3}^{C}\}+1
steps with the trianing distribution, where
T_{3}^{B}
and
T_{3}^{C}
are defined in (
205
) and (
194
).
We let

E^{(k-1)}=(E,\widehat{O}^{(k-1)})

be the input at the
k
-th reasoning step, and define

\displaystyle x_{i}\coloneqq E^{(\widetilde{m})}(1:d_{1},i),\quad y_{i}\coloneqq E^{(\widetilde{m})}(d_{1}+1:2d_{1},i),\quad\forall i\in[\widetilde{N}+\widetilde{m}+1].

(456)

We let
\widehat{o}^{(k)}=\widehat{o}^{(k)}({\mathcal{T}})\in\mathbb{R}^{2d_{1}+d_{2}}
denote the output of the
k
-th reasoning step of the model at test time for all
k\in[2\widetilde{m}]
.
We define

\displaystyle\widehat{x}^{(k)}\coloneqq\widehat{o}^{(k)}(1:d_{1}),\quad\widehat{y}^{(k)}\coloneqq\widehat{o}^{(k)}(d_{1}+1:2d_{1}),\quad\widehat{z}^{(k)}\coloneqq\widehat{o}^{(k)}(2d_{1}+1:2d_{1}+d_{2}),

(457)

and let

\displaystyle x^{(k)}\coloneqq o^{(k)}(1:d_{1}),\quad y^{(k)}\coloneqq o^{(k)}(d_{1}+1:2d_{1}),\quad z^{(k)}\coloneqq o^{(k)}(2d_{1}+1:2d_{1}+d_{2})

(458)

denote the label of the
k
-th reasoning step.

By the same argument as Lemma
24
, we know that at each reasoning step
k
, there exist
\left\{\beta_{i}^{(k)}\right\}_{i=1}^{\widetilde{N}+k}
,
\left\{\gamma_{i}^{(k)}\right\}_{i=1}^{\widetilde{N}+k}
such that

\displaystyle\left(\widehat{x}^{(k)\top},\widehat{y}^{(k)\top}\right)^{\top}=\sum_{i=1}^{\widetilde{N}+k}\beta_{i}^{(k)}\left(x_{i}^{\top},y_{i}^{\top}\right)^{\top},\quad\widehat{z}^{(k)}=\sum_{i=1}^{\widetilde{N}+k}\gamma_{i}^{(k)}z_{i},

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{\widetilde{N}+k}\beta_{i}^{(k)}=\sum_{i=1}^{\widetilde{N}+k}\gamma_{i}^{(k)}=1,\quad\beta_{i}^{(k)},\gamma_{i}^{(k)}\geq 0.

(459)

Especially, we let
\beta_{\star}^{(k)}
denote the proportions of
(x^{(k)\top},y^{(k)\top})^{\top}
in
(\widehat{x}^{(k)\top},\widehat{y}^{(k)\top})^{\top}
,
and let
\gamma_{\star}^{(k)}
denote the proportions of
z^{(k)}
in
\widehat{z}^{(k)}
.
We let

\displaystyle\alpha^{(k)}\coloneqq\min\left\{\beta_{\star}^{(k)},\gamma_{\star}^{(k)}\right\},\quad\delta^{(k)}\coloneqq\max\left\{1-\beta_{\star}^{(k)},1-\gamma_{\star}^{(k)}\right\}.

(460)

We define

\displaystyle\mu_{i}^{(k)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq x_{i}^{\top}B_{1}y^{(k-1)}+y_{i}^{\top}B_{2}x^{(k-1)}+z_{i}^{\top}B_{3}z^{(k-1)},

(461a)

\displaystyle\mu_{\star}^{(k)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq x^{(k)\top}B_{1}y^{(k-1)}+y^{(k)\top}B_{2}x^{(k-1)}+z^{(k)\top}B_{3}z^{(k-1)},

(461b)

\displaystyle\nu_{i}^{(k)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq x_{i}^{\top}C_{1}y^{(k-1)}+y_{i}^{\top}C_{2}x^{(k-1)}+z_{i}^{\top}C_{3}z^{(k-1)},

(461c)

\displaystyle\nu_{\star}^{(k)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq x^{(k)\top}C_{1}y^{(k-1)}+y^{(k)\top}C_{2}x^{(k-1)}+z^{(k)\top}C_{3}z^{(k-1)}.

(461d)

We also define for some absolute constant
C>0
,

\displaystyle v\coloneqq C(\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}),

(462)

where
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}
are defined in (
185
) and (
186
). By Lemma
17
and Lemma
20
we can see that there exists
C>0
such that for any
i,j,k,l\in[S]
, and any
p,q\in\{0,1\}
,

\displaystyle\left|a_{i}^{\top}B_{1}a_{j}+a_{k}^{\top}B_{2}a_{l}+s_{p}^{\top}B_{3}s_{q}\right|\leq v^{(k)},

(463)

\displaystyle\left|a_{i}^{\top}C_{1}a_{j}+a_{k}^{\top}C_{2}a_{l}+s_{p}^{\top}C_{3}s_{q}\right|\leq v^{(k)}.

(464)

We set
C
to make the above relations hold.
We define

\displaystyle\forall k\in[2\widetilde{m}],\quad\delta^{(k)}_{1}=1-\beta_{\star}^{(k)},

\displaystyle\quad\delta^{(k)}_{2}=1-\gamma_{\star}^{(k)},

(465)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\delta}_{1}^{(k)}\coloneqq\max_{s\in[k]}\left\{\delta^{(s)}_{1}\right\},

\displaystyle\quad\widetilde{\delta}_{2}^{(k)}\coloneqq\max_{s\in[k]}\left\{\delta^{(s)}_{2}\right\},

(466)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\beta}_{\star}^{(k)}\coloneqq\min_{s\in[k]}\left\{\beta_{\star}^{(s)}\right\},

\displaystyle\quad\widetilde{\gamma}_{\star}^{(k)}\coloneqq\min_{s\in[k]}\left\{\gamma_{\star}^{(s)}\right\},

(467)

and

\displaystyle\delta_{1}\coloneqq\frac{N+2m-1}{\exp\left(0.46\mu_{1}^{(t)}\right)+N+2m-1}\leq\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon}{6m}},\quad\delta_{2}\coloneqq\frac{N}{\exp\left(0.47\mu_{2}\right)+N}\leq\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon}{6m}},

(468)

where the inequalities follow from (
205
), (
194
) and that
t\geq\max\{T_{3}^{B},T_{3}^{C}\}+1
.
We first prove by induction that

\displaystyle\forall k\in[2\widetilde{m}],\quad\widetilde{\delta}^{(k)}_{1}\leq 2\max\left\{\frac{\widetilde{N}+2\widetilde{m}-1}{N+2m-1},1\right\}\delta_{1},\quad\widetilde{\delta}^{(k)}_{2}\leq 2\max\left\{\frac{\widetilde{N}}{N},1\right\}\delta_{2}.

(469)

We define

\displaystyle a\coloneqq 2\max\left\{\frac{\widetilde{N}+2\widetilde{m}-1}{N+2m-1},1\right\}.

(470)

When
k=1
, we have

\displaystyle\delta_{1}^{(1)}

\displaystyle\leq 1-\frac{\exp\left(\mu_{\star}^{(1)}\right)}{\exp\left(\mu_{\star}^{(1)}\right)+\sum_{i=1\atop i\neq\star}^{\widetilde{N}+1}\exp\left(\mu_{i}^{(1)}\right)}\leq\frac{\widetilde{N}}{\widetilde{N}+\exp\left(0.46\mu_{1}\right)}\overset{\eqref{eq:delta_generalization}}{\leq}\max\left\{\frac{\widetilde{N}}{N+2m-1},1\right\}\delta_{1}.

(471)

where we use
\star
to denote the index of
\mu_{\star}^{(1)}
, and the second inequality follows from Lemma
21
.
Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\delta_{2}^{(1)}\leq\max\left\{\frac{\widetilde{N}}{N},1\right\}\delta_{2}.

(472)

Thus (
469
) holds for
k=1
.
Now we assume (
469
) holds for
k
(
1\leq k\leq 2\widetilde{m}-1
), and prove it for
k+1
.

We have

\displaystyle\beta_{\star}^{(k+1)}\geq\frac{\exp\left(\left(\widetilde{\beta}_{\star}^{(k)}\right)^{2}\mu_{\star}^{(k)}-\left(1-\left(\widetilde{\beta}_{\star}^{(k)}\right)^{2}\right)v\right)}{\exp\left(\left(\widetilde{\beta}_{\star}^{(k)}\right)^{2}\mu_{\star}^{(k)}-\left(1-\left(\widetilde{\beta}_{\star}^{(k)}\right)^{2}\right)v\right)+\sum_{i=1\atop i\neq\star}^{\widetilde{N}+k}\exp\left(\left(\widetilde{\beta}_{\star}^{(k)}\right)^{2}\mu_{i}^{(k)}+\left(1-\left(\widetilde{\beta}_{\star}^{(k)}\right)^{2}\right)v\right)},

(473)

indicating

\displaystyle\delta_{1}^{(k+1)}\leq\frac{\widetilde{N}+k-1}{\widetilde{N}+k-1+\exp\bigg(0.46\left(\widetilde{\beta}_{\star}^{(k)}\right)^{2}\mu_{1}-2\left(1-\left(\widetilde{\beta}_{\star}^{(k)}\right)^{2}\right)v\bigg)}.

(474)

Note that

\displaystyle 1-\left(\widetilde{\beta}_{\star}^{(k)}\right)^{2}=\bigg(1-\widetilde{\beta}_{\star}^{(k)}\bigg)\bigg(1+\widetilde{\beta}_{\star}^{(k)}\bigg)\leq 2\widetilde{\delta}^{(k)}_{1}\leq 2a\delta_{1},

(475)

where the last inequality follows from induction hypothesis and the definition of
a
(c.f. (
470
)).
Plugging this into (
474
) we have

\displaystyle\delta_{1}^{(k+1)}\leq\frac{\widetilde{N}+k-1}{\widetilde{N}+k-1+\exp\bigg(0.46\mu_{1}-\underbrace{2a\delta_{1}\left(2v+0.46\mu_{1}\right)}_{(a)}\bigg)},

(476)

On the other hand, by the definition of
\delta_{1}
and
\delta_{2}
we have

\displaystyle\mu_{1}=\frac{1}{0.46}\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta_{1}}-1\right),\quad\mu_{2}=\frac{1}{0.47}\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta_{2}}-1\right).

(477)

and

\displaystyle\delta_{1}\leq\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon}{6m}},\quad\delta_{2}\leq\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon}{6m}}.

(478)

Also observe that

x\log x\rightarrow 0_{+}
as
x\rightarrow 0_{+}
.
Thus we could set
\epsilon_{1}=\epsilon_{1}(m,\widetilde{N},\widetilde{m})
small enough such that (a) in (
476
) is smaller than
\log 2
for any
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{1}]
. Thus by (
476
) we have

\displaystyle\delta_{1}^{(k+1)}\leq\frac{\widetilde{N}+k-1}{\widetilde{N}+k-1+\frac{1}{2}\exp\bigg(0.46\mu_{1}\bigg)}\leq 2\frac{\widetilde{N}+k-1}{\widetilde{N}+k-1+\exp\bigg(0.46\mu_{1}\bigg)}\leq 2\max\left\{\frac{\widetilde{N}+k-1}{N+2m-1},1\right\}\delta_{1}.

(479)

Similarly, there exists
\epsilon_{2}=\epsilon_{2}(m,\widetilde{N},\widetilde{m})
small enough such that

\displaystyle\delta_{2}^{(k+1)}\leq 2\max\left\{\frac{\widetilde{N}}{N},1\right\}\delta_{2}.

(480)

We let
\epsilon_{0}=\min\{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}\}
, then (
469
) holds for
k+1
.

Following a similar calculation as in the proof of Lemma
22
and Lemma
18
, we can show that

\displaystyle\Delta_{x}^{(k)}\leq\left(\delta_{1}^{(k)}\right)^{2},\quad\Delta_{y}^{(k)}\leq\left(\delta_{1}^{(k)}\right)^{2},\quad\Delta_{z}^{(k)}\leq\left(\delta_{2}^{(k)}\right)^{2},

(481)

and thus

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathsf{test}}({\mathcal{T}};\theta)\leq 4\max\left\{\left(\frac{\widetilde{N}+2\widetilde{m}-1}{N+2m-1}\right)^{2},\left(\frac{\widetilde{N}}{N}\right)^{2},1\right\}\epsilon.

(482)
